The kill is the same trip event watched or unwatched (`OutpostLedger.HuntKill`), so both spend the same. The carcass and the haul home are unchanged. (Before bows existed a spear hunter with arrows loosed one per kill; that is gone — arrows need a bow now.)
- **Defence.** At the alarm hands take **spears first, then bows** (a bow only while the camp has arrows); one store bow per posted tower lookout is kept back for him. A bow hand shoots any raider within **18 m** — over the wall too — every **1.5 s**, hitting **70 %** for **1.5** damage (a raider has 3 hp; about 3 arrows a raider, in line with the landing volley's 2), otherwise he holds the gate like a spear hand. A **tower lookout with the camp's bow shoots first**, from **26 m**, and his arrows never draw the raider to the tower. Spear hands still close to melee. The bow walks home at all clear like a spear, worn by its kills. The landing volley (5 arrows per manned tower, 2 arrows a raider) and the away raid are unchanged and need no bow. Knobs: `RaidFightTuning` "Bows".
- **At sea.** Every **3 s** each able hand with a bow in the hold looses one arrow from the hold at the nearest raider hull within **40 m** (the gun lock first): archers = min(bows aboard, able crew). A hit (**60 %**) banks **0.1** of a round shot; every whole shot's worth lands through the ordinary hit, so ten arrow hits = one cannonball. Seen as an arcing volley, splinters on a hit, splashes on a miss. Knobs: `RaidFightTuning` "Bows at sea". There are no boarders yet.

**Storage is the hub, and nothing is worked without an order (2026-09-23, Kevin's storage-hub rules).** Before a Storage/Storehouse stands, the camp's goods sit in a square beside the campfire; once one is built, they move inside it (the visual may cap at extreme counts, the true count never does). **Every gathered unit goes to storage** — a gatherer does not carry straight to a station. When storage is full, gatherers stop gathering that good and find another task *(superseded 2026-10-03: island storage is never full; see "Island stores: infinite stacking")*. A production station (sawmill, quarry, fletcher, kitchen, blacksmith) owns its own stock — an input bay, a one-job bench and an output rack — sized to the station (sawmill 6 logs in / 12 planks out, quarry 5 stone in / 12 brick out) — and **nothing on the bench is worked without a player order** (make N, or repeat until stopped). Hauling is by the armful: when a raw bay runs empty, an idle villager or the station's own stationed worker fetches from storage; if storage has none, the stationed worker gathers it himself when the raw is gatherable, otherwise he waits ("waiting for X (made at Y)"); finished goods move from the rack to storage when the rack is full, when a hauler is idle, or when the worker runs out of raw to keep working (*hauling superseded 2026-10-02: see "Runners and full loads" below*). A builder draws from storage first and station racks second. Station stock counts toward camp totals, and every building always shows its true stock — no piles that are actually empty, no goods that exist but do not display.

**Station spots, Melvor-style (2026-09-30, Kevin).** Every crafting station has one or more **spots**, each running its own selected recipe **at the same time**, all tended by the station's ONE worker at the full rate each (his effort is not divided; the timers run only while he stands at the station). The kitchen has a **Grill** (grilled fish, grilled meat, roast carrots, baked potato, ship's biscuit) and a **Cauldron** (veg stew, hunter's stew, fish pie, bread); the forge a **Smelter** (iron) and a **Forge** (spear, saw blade, tools, iron spear); every other station one spot named after itself (`Recipe.spot`, `StationSpots`). **Select a recipe on a spot** and it runs until an input runs out or its output has nowhere to go, then **auto-pauses with a reason** — *"waiting for onion"*, *"store full of grilled fish"*, *"no cook"*, *"needs the fire at II"* — and resumes by itself when that clears; **Stop** clears the spot (an unfinished batch's inputs go back to the bay). This replaces the per-station order with amount chips (∞/5/10/20/Stop) and the phase-2 standing-order queue as the player-facing model; a selected spot is "∞". Each spot has its own bench; the input bay and the output rack stay the station's and are **shared** by its spots at full capacity (slot counts are still capacity). Everything else holds: deliveries count on arrival, the ledger is deterministic, idle hands and the worker haul every spot's inputs in and the rack out, an input made at another station with none in storage waits with its reason (no auto-chaining), station stock counts in camp totals. Old saves: the station's order (or queue lines — a count keeps its count, repeat/keep become ∞) lands on the spot its recipe belongs to, and a batch on the old bench moves onto that spot's bench. Code: `OutpostLedger.Spots.cs` (`SelectRecipe`, `StopSpot`, `RefreshSpots`), `StationSpots.cs`, `StationStock.spots`.

**Island stores: infinite stacking; the containers are the picture (2026-10-03).** Kevin first approved the storage-slot containers preview with *capacity = visible slots*, then the same day replaced the capacity rule: *"remove storage limits. infinite stacking is allowed."* So an **island** store never refuses goods — gatherers, hunters, farmers and runners never stall or rest because the store is full, a farm harvests its whole yield, nothing is set down beside the store for lack of room, and there is no "Store full" alert. **The ship is not an island store:** *"the ship will not have infinite storage"* — every hold and cargo limit is unchanged; only the island side of a transfer stopped refusing. Bare ground with no fire still keeps nothing. **No ground piles** still holds: the ring of stacks by the fire, the stacks beside the store hut, the kitchen fish pile and crate are gone; the only loose goods on the ground are a fallen hand's dropped load and the ship's deck cargo. The goods are **shown** in authored container slots — a slot holds one bundle of ONE resource in fill steps, and once every slot of a family is full it stays shown full while the true count keeps rising (the sheets show the real number). Seven families, each slot claimable by any resource of its family: **log cradle** (Timber, 5 a slot), **stone crib** (Stone, Ore, Brick, 10), **board bearers** (Boards, FineBoards, 10), **sacks** (Potato, Carrot, Onion, Wheat, Apple, Flour, Spice, foraged Food, **12**), **hang beam** (Fish, Meat, Hide, 4 a peg), **dish shelf** (every cooked dish and ship's biscuit, **12 a tray**), **gear rack** (Arrows 12, Spear / Iron spear / Bow 4, Tools 4, Saw blade 2, Iron 6). Slots — **store hut L1:** logs 4, stone 3, boards 3, sacks 6, pegs 6, dish trays 4, gear 4; **fire cache** (a pegged groundsheet, 3.2 × 2.3 m, and a tripod by the campfire; it still counts once a store hut stands, and the hut fills first): 2/1/1/2/2/1/1. A level adds no slots: **store hut level 2 is about its runners (4 instead of 2)**. *(The storehouse's provisional twice-a-hut slots went with the Storehouse, 2026-10-04.)* **The one place the slots still act as a limit:** an idle hand's "top the store up from the field" errand stops once that resource's family has every visible slot full (fire cache + huts), and he rests; assigned gatherers, hunters, farmers and runners keep going without limit. Code: `World/StorageSlots.cs` (the table), `OutpostLedger.RoomFor` (unlimited) / `SlotCapacityOf` / `SlotRoomFor` / `Fill01`, `Outpost.StoreSlotsNow`, `World/StorageSlotView.cs` (empty `Stock_<Family>_NN` anchors in the building FBX; steps from the shared `Resources/Kits/StorageL1/StorageFillKit` kit as `<Family>__Fill_<k>` / `<Family>__Fill_<Res>_<k>`, `Fire<Family>__…` first under the FireCache root, instantiated on first need).

**Build sites: whole armfuls capped at need, and trip time is the walk (2026-09-23, same phone playtest, "we finetune later").** A build site's hunger is exact: a builder brings the whole armful the site can still use, net of every load already walking to it — two haulers can never both bring the last 3 boards, and the site can never hold more than its cost. The counts on the site's line are the true delivered **whole units**, never a fraction. **Hammering — and the 0–100% progress bar with it — waits for every material to be fully in** (5/5 wood, 3/3 stone, whatever the plan costs) *and* the plot cleared of whatever trees/rocks stood on it; it is 0% until both are true, never a number that creeps ahead of the pile. None of this is a timer: **trip time is the real walking distance there and back.** Every trip — a station haul, a site delivery, a plain gather — runs drop-off → pickup → drop-off: `2 × leg × PathFactor (1.15) / WalkMetresPerSecond (2.6 m/s) + HandleSeconds (3 s)`, plus cutting time at the pickup when it's the island itself. The store leg goes to the storage building once one stands, else the campfire square; the source leg is the camp's own measured distance to its nearest standing tree/rock. **Kevin's playtest dials — his numbers to test with, not balance, tune later:** wood 5 s/log, stone 8 s, ore 10 s; clearing one tree off a plot is 5 s of builder time, one rock is 8 s. **Plain Gather orders are trips too** — store → source, cut/pick an armful, back to store, deposit — not a per-day accrual; with the store full a gatherer says so, helps haul for the stations or helps build until there's room, and resumes gathering on his own. **Carried loads are visible**: a hand hauling 3 logs shows three logs in his arms (capped at 6 for the eye). All of it — sites, stations, plain gathering — is one ledger's arithmetic (`OutpostLedger`), so a watched camp and an unwatched one land on exactly the same books at any tick size (D2), and the villagers you see are miming the trips the ledger already booked, never driving them. *Superseded 2026-09-27 (`docs/DELIVERY-ON-ARRIVAL.md`):* the trip FORMULA above (`2 × leg × PathFactor / speed + handle`) no longer books anything — trips are actually walked (by the body when watched, by an invisible walker when not) and goods count on arrival. What stays a timer is only stationary work: cutting at the source (the tuning asset's seconds per unit), the ~1 s pickup stoop, clearing, hammering (only with every material in and a builder on the site), the bench recipe. What comes off a cleared plot is carried to the store; a farmhand's harvest is carried in by the armful.

**Runners and full loads (2026-10-02, approved; supersedes the hauling sentences of "Storage is the hub" above and the 2026-09-27 idle-hand ladder "haul to blueprints → build → gather").** A **runner** is a hand assigned to the store hut (2 / 4 / 6 per store hut at levels 1 / 2 / 3 since 2026-10-04, see "The store hut is runner progression"); he pushes a **wheelbarrow** (~3 armfuls: 6 logs, 8 stone, 10 boards, 16 small goods) and does all the camp's logistics — dropped loads, ship transfers, stations' bays (an empty one first, then pre-stocked while the bench works), blocked racks, blueprint materials, full racks home, rack output straight to a bay or site that wants it, and a backhaul of the rack after he fills a bay; within the same priority the request that has **waited longest** goes first (in steps of 0.1 work-day), and only equal waits go nearest first (2026-10-03, so one busy station cannot starve another copy or another station). With nothing to carry he waits at the store (*"Runner, waiting"*), which is not idle. **While a runner is on the island**, stationed workers stay at their benches (*"Waiting for a runner"*, "waiting for hide · runners bringing it"; **never a walk to the store or its piles** — Kevin 2026-10-02 retired the ~10-quanta self-fetch fallback; a worker still moves his own rack to his own bay, and still cuts a raw the store has none of, since no runner cuts); an idle bench's input outranks even a dropped load, a runner whose store-bound load no longer fits sets it down by the store as a dropped load and runs on, and each runner waits on his own spot by the hut, builders fetch their own site's materials from the store and racks, runners or not (*Kevin 2026-10-03: "builders fetch themselves"* — runners help stock sites, they are not the only path), and cut/quarry straight to a site what nobody has; free hands build → top the store up from the field → rest at the fire with **No job** (2026-10-03: "Idle" split into Reserve / No job / Builder — waiting for a material, see the decisions log). **Without runners** hauling works as before, under the same **full-load rules for everyone**: a trip leaves with a full armful unless it finishes the job, empties the source or fills the destination; racks go home only with a full load on them, when they block the bench, or in the work day's last hour; bays are only pre-stocked once at least half a bay (or a full armful) fits. A bay **short of one batch** takes a **part load** — whatever the store or a rack has, and a rack load it has no room for in full is tipped in as far as it fits, the rest staying on the rack, instead of going to the store (2026-10-03); the barrow shows the fewer items. Priority overall: emergencies → manned stations kept supplied → food safety → blueprints → pre-stocking → full racks → store top-up from the field → rest. Code: `OutpostLedger.Runners.cs`, `FindHaulerChore`; the ledger decides, bodies mime, live and offline alike. **2026-10-03 (Kevin):** crew dropped at a camp land **Idle** (no default "Gather timber" job), so the idle ladder and the food draft can use them; there is **no pouting** at all, not even after a death.

**The store hut is runner progression; the Storehouse is merged into it (Kevin 2026-10-04).** Kevin, seeing his Storehouse beside the store hut: *"merge them into the right building. Remove the building I built on the other side of the wall."* The runner progression approved for the Storehouse on 2026-10-03 (*"the more you upgrade it, the more / better runners you get"*) is now the **store hut's own levels**, and the Storehouse is gone from the game (build list, plan, upgrade steps, caps, slots, sheets). A hand on the Work order at a store hut is a runner (as before).

| Store hut level | its runner posts | perk for EVERY runner on the island | cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| L1 (built) | 2 | — | 16 timber + 2 stone (unchanged) |
| L2 (fire II) | 4 | barrow +1 armful, jog 10% faster | 6 brick + 2 fine boards (unchanged) |
| L3 (fire II for now — fire III does not exist yet) | 6 | barrow +2 armfuls, jog 20% faster | 14 brick + 8 fine boards (the old Storehouse L3 bill; provisional) |

Level 3 is new for the store hut; until it has art it shows the standard level look (the gold trim of level 2). Perks come from the **highest-level** store hut on the camp (a second copy adds its posts, never a second perk) and derive from building levels alone, so nothing new is saved. **Barrow loads** (base / +1 / +2 armfuls): logs 6 / 8 / 10, stone 8 / 11 / 14, boards 10 / 14 / 18, brick 12 / 16 / 20, small goods 16 / 20 / 24, arrows 36 / 48 / 60, saw blades 2 / 2 / 3; fish still go "the whole box at once" — so a level 1 store hut hauls exactly as before. **Jog:** 2.04 / 2.24 / 2.45 m/s (×1.3 on a road: 2.65 / 2.92 / 3.18), in the books and on the body alike; the Run clip plays at most at rate 1.56, inside its 2.0 window. The store hut's sheet says it: upgrade rows "+2 runner posts · barrow +1 armful · jog 10% faster", and a perk line on its Runners card ("Every runner on this island: …", or below level 2 what level 2 brings), wrapped, never cut. **Old saves (one-time load repair, Kevin's explicit request — the single exception to "saved buildings never move"):** every Storehouse in a camp is taken down before the camp is raised (its body never spawns) and its whole cost goes back into that camp's store: 25 timber + 4 stone for the building (+25% a copy after the first, rounded up), plus 8 brick + 4 fine boards if it had reached level 2 and a further 14 brick + 8 fine boards for level 3; a Storehouse blueprint still queued is dropped and what was carried to it comes back. Its runners move onto free store hut runner posts (still runners), else go Idle (not the reserve), a load already in the barrow going into the store. Nothing else on the camp moves. Idempotent, one log line per camp. Code: `OutpostLedger.Runners.cs` (`IsRunnerPost`, `StoreRunners`, `PerksAt`, `RunnerPerks`, `StoreHutLevel`), `OutpostLedger.StorehouseMerge.cs` (`MergeStorehouses`, from `Outpost.Adopt`), `Res.BarrowArmful(r, extra)`, `VillagerGaits.BarrowAt`. Self-test: `StorehouseMergeSelfTest.Run()`.

*Superseded 2026-10-04 (above):* the 2026-10-03 "Storehouse is runner progression" design — a separate Storehouse with its own 2 / 4 / 6 posts stacking on the huts' 2 / 4, a camp-wide perk from level 1, built at camps with a placeholder look.

**Design guarantee.** `RecipeGraph.Validate()` walks every fire level, plan, recipe and ship rung and refuses them all if anything — from a fresh camp starting with only the ground to gather from — cannot be reached in order. If a build breaks the rule (e.g., iron spears needed before iron is made, or a building asking for something only that building makes), the probe says so at load in dev/editor builds and via `unity cmd eval`. The generated doc `docs/PRODUCTION-CHAINS.md` (one generated per build; never check in) lists every resource, every recipe, every fire level and every building upgrade so the team sees the shape of it all at once.

The **player** sees recipes through a station sheet — pick a recipe, place an order (a count, or repeat until stopped), watch the bay fill from storage, watch the hands work, watch the output land on the rack and move to storage. The sheet names what is locked and why, so the player reads the ladder forward: *"I need a spear to hunt, I need a blacksmith to make one, I need boards and stone first."*

### Island UI structure (2026-09-30)
Approved blueprint (Kevin, 2026-09-30): a new structure on the existing UI Toolkit sheet engine, phone portrait and one thumb first. Phase 1 (thumb bar, one-strip Build, labelled placing), phase 2 (the Next card and the goal chain) and phase 3 (every shortfall, mood reason and alert carries its fix button; no-beds, store-full and walled-off alerts; the grave is placed from the thumb bar with no Cancel) are built; phase 4 (one home per topic: the Camp sheet replaces the ☰ drawer and Overview, Food absorbs the Larder, Workers absorbs People, the Backpack is the only cargo and store screen, the Ship sheet is crew + Cast off) is built; phases 5-6 follow, each on the phone before the next.

**Layers, one job each.** *Top bar* = how are we doing (backpack · mood · working/total · food days with trend · day); a tap explains a number, never hides an action. *World* = the things themselves; tap a building, hand, blueprint or the ship to open its sheet; no icons over world objects. *Bottom (thumb)* = what can I do: the **Next card** above **Camp · Build · Ship**, the only always-on controls. *Sheets* = one topic each, one main action in the thumb row. The alert strip under the top bar shows ONE chip plus, from two alerts up, a **"N problems"** chip (2026-10-03; was "+N" into Camp) that opens the **Problems list**: every alert in plain words, station first where a station is stuck (*"Sawmill · needs logs (Yara)"*, *"Kitchen · no cook"*, *"Sawmill · waiting on the runners for logs"*, *"Store full of boards …"*, *"Finch lost the sawmill post …"*, *"Builders short of stone"*), each row one tap to the building's or hand's sheet; a short sheet that hugs its rows (at most half the phone screen) with Camp · Close. Hidden when nothing is stuck. An alert the Next card already shows is not repeated in the strip's single chip.

**The 10 rules (condensed).** 1 One main action per sheet, in the thumb row. 2 Every problem shows its fix as a button. 3 One home per topic; others link, never copy. 4 Information only where it is acted on. 5 Controls in the bottom 40 %; the top is for reading. 6 Always a way out: every mode has a labelled Cancel. 7 Teach by doing: the Next card is the tutorial, each step is the real action. 8 The ladder is visible: locked things say what unlocks them; NEW badges once. 9 Same words everywhere (hand; Assign, Build, Gather, Raise). 10 Short is still placeable: an unaffordable blueprint can be placed, and idle hands gather what is missing.

**The Next card** (`UI/Sheets/NextCard.cs`): one card directly above the thumb bar, the whole card one button. It shows, in order: a pinned goal ("YOUR GOAL", `GoalPin`; replaces the old GoalBar) while incomplete; else the first open goal-chain step ("NEXT · STEP n OF m"); else the most urgent camp alert ("NEEDS YOU", its fix the tap); else nothing (no filler). Hidden while a sheet, the Camp drawer or placement is up. Before a camp exists it stands alone in the bar's slot as step 1.

**The goal chain** (`UI/Sheets/GoalChainSteps.cs`, one data list): derived from the save every refresh, never scripted by time. The first step whose done test fails is current; done steps and steps whose building is locked at this fire are skipped, so an old save lands on the right step. Tapping the card performs the step (build steps open Build with the plan marked GOAL; a placed blueprint shows its progress and opens its site).
1. Make camp (place the campfire). 2. Build a hut. 3. Grow food (a farm or fishing hut, manned). 4. Build a store hut. 5. Build a sawmill. 6. Give the sawmill a worker. 7. Make boards. 8. Build a forge. 9. Make a stone spear. 10. Send a hunter. 11. Raise the fire to II (20 boards · 12 stone · 4 hide). Then: the most urgent alert.

**The landing party (2026-09-30, replaces the gather party).** Kevin at Island_3: *"horrible ui, it doesn't fit the rest of the game and it's structured in a way that makes no sense. only room for two resources ... what if i want to hunt, or explore?"* Approved mockups "7 · Landing party: Explore (fog)" / "7b · Gather". Stopped off an island with no camp, the anchor prompt's **Landing party** row (where "⛏ Send gather party" was) opens `LandingPartySheet`: a half-height card in the house look (it hugs its content, the island stays visible), header "Landing party · Anchored off Island_3 · 4 aboard" (Gather: "hold 23/34") ~~with an "N% explored" pill~~ (gone with the fog, 2026-10-03), ☰ (pause menu) and ✕, then **two order cards, Gather and Hunt** (Explore was a third until 2026-10-03: *Kevin: "remove the Explore card"*). **No fog since 2026-10-03** (Kevin: *"remove the fog of war"*; decisions log): every island is fully visible, so "found" below now means "on the island" (`World/IslandInventory.cs`), nothing is limited to revealed ground, the "Explore first" buttons are plain text and the sheet opens on Gather. ~~**Explore:** ON THIS ISLAND chips (was FOUND SO FAR from the fog), one line of what happens, WHO GOES portraits ("spear" / "bow · 8" / "unarmed" / "at the helm"); the hands walk inland for **120 s** (`GatherParty.ExploreSeconds`), each on his own fanned bearing, every stop a walkable straight line spread from the others' (until 2026-10-03: toward the most unrevealed ground, revealing 25 m round them), and walk home along their own trail. **Since the fog went, Explore yields nothing** (it never spawned or unlocked anything; it only opened ground) — kept, reporting "Nothing new found", pending Kevin's call. **Mild risk, never death:** the first time an explorer comes within 18 m of a live boar it is rolled once, **35 %** unarmed party / **5 %** if anyone carries a spear or bow; a hurt hand is brought home and is off station 120 s (the ship's rescue-rest, logged Downed).~~ **Explore removed 2026-10-03** (Kevin; decisions log): no card, no order, no 120 s walk, no boar roll on a walk, no ON THIS ISLAND chip row, no "Nothing new found". **Gather:** every found kind as a 4-wide icon tile, HOW MUCH 5 / 10 / All N (N = hold room as far as the reachable sources go), WHO GOES name pills; **2026-10-03 (Kevin: a massive island showed no food, and "All N" could not deliver N):** wild **berry** bushes are a Gather tile ("berries", `Res.Food` raw, one a bush, 5 s each; picked bushes go bare and are booked by name in the island's ledger, regrowing in 20 days; a camp's own field count never includes a bush a party holds); a tile's figure is exactly what the party can carry off — every reachable source on the whole island (`IslandInventory.Sources`, one list for tile, "All N" and the trip), and the party keeps re-standing nodes nearest-first as it uses them up (48 trees / 60 rocks / 48 bushes standing at once is a budget, not a cap); the 2026-09-27 trip (limited to revealed sources until 2026-10-03). **The whole island since 2026-10-03** (Kevin: *"the landing party reaches the whole island, nothing moves, far = longer trip"*): no 80 m cap; a source counts when a hand can walk to it — a straight walkable line, else a route over the island's walk grid (`CampPath`, one flood from the landing, `CampPath.Flood.cs`), else not at all; nothing is moved toward the landing (the Ore outcrop and an Ore/Stone island's seam stay where they are); a far source is a longer walk, and a tile whose nearest source is past 80 m shows "far · m:ss" (round trip at shore pace + one armful); "cut off" = no way to walk there. **Hunt:** the found herds as tiles; only armed hands can be picked (weapons dealt from the HOLD: iron spear, stone spear, then bow while arrows last); each hunter stalks the nearest beast (revealed only, until 2026-10-03), jabs (2 m) or shoots (12 m), shoulders it and carries Meat + Hide into the hold; one beast per hunter, a bow kill spends one arrow, the camp's hunting-accident roll applies (hurt, not dead). Nobody armed: the fix is plain text ("Nobody is armed. Take a spear or a bow and arrows aboard at a camp."), no primary. ONE primary says exactly what happens ("Send 2 to gather 10 ore", "Send Pip to hunt goats"); while a party is ashore the card is its progress and **Call them back**; back aboard, a toast (and the card) says what they brought ("6 timber aboard"; until 2026-10-03 also what they found: "Found ore and a cairn"). **Also:** the cannons' IMGUI port/stbd fire chips show only with an enemy in range (a held lock, or a hostile within 1.5x gun reach, min 60 m; Q/E still fire on a desk); off a fresh island the rail's "Ledger" and "Menu" chips stand down, and the pause menu is the landing sheet's ☰ (or Esc). Numbers PROVISIONAL, unplayed.

**Sea combat controls (2026-09-30, phase 6; approved mockup "8b · Sea: combat").** The IMGUI fire chips (`CannonBattery`) and the round lock button (`CombatLock`) are retired for `UI/Sheets/CombatHud.cs`: a red **target chip** under the sea top bar ("RAIDER · 120 m · locked", or "RAIDER · 140 m" with only a candidate) and **one combat row** just above the helm row: **◀ Fire port** (bright ice when a gun on that side is loaded, dark while it reloads, reload bar inside), **Lock / Release** ("Lock · 120 m" with a candidate in reach, "Release" ember while held, amber as the lock slips out of range), **Fire stbd ▶**; a one-line note above it ("Locked guns fire by themselves · 2 archers shooting"). Nobody on a side's guns: that side reads as plain text ("No crew"), both sides: "No crew on the guns". Shown only with an enemy to fight (a held lock, a hostile within 1.5x gun reach, or a lock candidate in reach). The row is a blocked rect (`UIBlocker.SheetBlocked`), so the helm stick and world taps ignore it; tapping an enemy hull still locks it; Q / E / Space and auto-fire while locked are unchanged. The corner brackets on the locked target stay IMGUI (world-anchored). Numbers and layout PROVISIONAL, unplayed.

**Phase 6: the last camp-view IMGUI moves to UI Toolkit (2026-09-30).** Four leftovers, same house look, nothing new to learn. (1) **Return from a voyage**: the ship arriving back shows one toast card under the chart, "WHILE YOU WERE GONE · 2.3 DAYS" over a one-line gist ("Raised a hut · Bo joined the camp · gathered 12 timber"), 12 s; a tap opens the full report on the same `AwaySheet` the time-away "Welcome back" uses (`AwaySummary.FromAbsence`: Events, Made, Gathered, Built; titled "While you were gone", in game days). (2) **The raid verdict** after the raiders have gone ("The raiders fled with nothing" / "All clear.") is an ember-rimmed "RAID" card on the same toast (`PartyReportToast`), 8 s, tap to dismiss; not while the Raid sheet is open. (3) **The carry line**: holding a villager, a pill in the thumb lane just above the bar / Next card says what letting go does ("Bo — cut timber", "back aboard") or, ember rim and a cross, why it refuses ("not in the sea"); it never takes a touch (`CarryPill`). On a desktop the same pill carries the empty-handed hover labels ("build", "blueprint"). (4) **Graves**: `GraveSheet`, a half-height hugging sheet; the story page (name, Day 3 – Day 9, BORN / DIED / LIVED, how they died, the three `LifeStory` sentences) with "‹ All graves" and Close, and the list page (newest first, 56-unit rows, a tap opens that story). Opened by a tap on a standing stone, right after a stone is laid, and from the dev LIFE panel. **Still IMGUI in camp view:** the Settings panel (Menu → Settings) and `RaidBanner`'s live warning / scoreline and Hide-everyone button on the classic HUD and at sea (the land HUD's RAID chip and `RaidSheet` replace them ashore).

**The sea HUD (2026-09-30, phase 6; approved mockups "8 · Sea: sailing" / "8c · Anchored off a fresh island").** The IMGUI sea HUD retires into the house look (`UI/Sheets/SeaHud.cs`, `Resources/UI/SeaHud.uss`, sized in the mockup's 390-wide design px like the combat row). **Top bar** (at sea and off an island with no camp; at her own camp the island bar is up): hold "12/34" with the bag (amber from 80 %, ember overloaded; tap = the Backpack's ship page, or its new **ship-only page** with no camp: the hold's tiles with no -/+, and Deck cargo) · hands aboard (tap = the Ship sheet) · the sea's word (calm ... wild, "BREAKERS" / "broaching" in ember; off a fresh island "Island_7 · 20% explored" instead — since the fog went (2026-10-03) just "Island_7" —, and no day) · Day N · ☰ (pause menu). The chart drops under it. **One alert chip + "+N"**: MAN OVERBOARD · name (tap = steer toward), Taking water 40% · 2 bailing / Hull 82% · repair (tap = Ship sheet), Overloaded · throw 5 over (tap = jettison 5). **The action card**: anything that can be done right now offers itself every frame (`SeaActions.Offer(priority, eyebrow, title, detail, tap, enabled, progress)`; rescue 300 > land/alongside 200 > other 100); the highest shows above the thumb in the Next card's look, ONE tap; an offer that is information (slow down, no free berth, a pull in progress) has no chevron and leaves the stick free. Castaways ("IN THE WATER · 60 m / Pull Edda aboard"), the man-overboard boarding timer and "steering to" live here. **Order strip** under way (2026-10-03, DREDGE controls; was the helm row with Oars and Ease, both gone): one slim line showing the LIVE drive ("slow ahead · 6.2 m/s", a live-throttle bar with the full-ahead tick and the burn stretch lit only while boosting), plus the round **⚡ boost button** at its right end; information only, so a thumb resting there starts the stick. The ring under the thumb is drawn in IMGUI (`SeaStick`). **First-use hints**: a faint dashed ring and "Drag to sail · let go to stop" in the boat zone until the stick is used once, then "Drag up here to look around · double-tap to recenter" in the camera zone (`GestureHints`, PlayerPrefs flags). **The Ship sheet works at sea** (header "at sea", no Cast off, a Repair pill that says "mend at anchor", plus **Home** (tap twice = sail home, the old ⌂ Home tab) and **Ledger** (the sea ledger drawer, whose rail button is gone)). Retired: `HoldChip`, `PauseChip`, `StatusHUD`'s panel, `HomeTab`, `Bilge`'s "over the side" prompt, `HelmInput`'s panel and `TouchHelm`'s readout; the salvage "+2 timber" is the notice toast. Unplayed.

## 7. MVP milestone list
Each milestone is a committable, testable slice. Sailing first; work backward.

1. **Boat on water** — greybox portrait scene: Gerstner ocean, buoyant hull, a one-thumb boat stick (up/down speed, left/right turn; release = engine off), constant wind. *Exit test: steering around swells is fun with zero art.*
2. **Wind & trim** — speed from wind angle + trim order; smoothness metric computed and visualized (debug).
3. **Sickness on one crew member** — meter driven by smoothness + time; green tint + puke-at-railing behavior; potion as a tap order.
4. **The voyage loop** — depart dock → reach island anchor → auto-gather → sail back → loot tally screen.
5. ~~**Mutiny & escalation**~~ — built 2026-08-17, **removed 2026-08-19**. Replaced by crew-as-labour: sickness costs you the ship's capability instead of triggering a scripted turn-back.
6. **Crew of three** — stations, orders, per-member sickness; traits as data.
7. **Camp screen economy** — menu-based village: spend loot on 2–3 buildings + a ship upgrade + potions that visibly change the next voyage.
8. **On-device build** — real phone, portrait, one-handed input polish pass. (Earlier if feel is in doubt — feel can only be judged on the phone.)

## 8. Out of scope (for now)
- ~~Combat: enemy ships, sea monsters.~~ Now in scope: raiders and cannons are built (§6), and the kraken is the first monster that fights back (§6 "The Kraken", 2026-10-03).
- Walkable 3D village.
- Breeding/genetics depth, multi-ship fleets, weather systems beyond wind, narrative/quests, monetization.

## 9. Open questions
- Which phone (iOS/Android) for device testing?
- Player-provided ship asset formats & import (pivots for rudder/sail rotation).
- Does the captain exist as a visible character on deck?

## 10. Decisions log
- 2026-10-05 — **Mine shaft: one villager mines stone out of a cliff face (Kevin).** Kevin: *"you can assign one villager to it he will walk in the mine shaft, and come back after x time and put stone or ore in the container outside. this structure can only be built on the side of a mountain / plateau on an island."* Decided: stone for now, ore in future; never runs dry; ~60 s underground, 4 stone a trip; the back of the model clips into the mountain and the ghost turns by itself like the dock; the model's baked-rock crate is replaced by the game's carry crate (2.5x) as the fill container; cost 8 timber, campfire level 1, copies 1/1/2/2 like the quarry; level 2 available but just tinted like the other buildings. Built on branch `mine-shaft`, UNPLAYED; L2 cost provisional. See the mine shaft paragraph in Islands.
- 2026-10-04 — **Chart: camp lines under the map, names beside islands (Kevin: "it's fine as is for now, we will fix it another time").** The chart's camp ledger lines left the map for tappable rows under it (place name, then the biggest pile · hands · days of food, the top bar's own number; Home first); island names sit beside their shapes within ~40 px, never over an outline, ring, mark or another name; unseen islets carry no word on the map (a tap reads "Unseen" and rings it). **PARKED by Kevin:** label placement polish — leader lines, and names that drift off small islands in a crowded archipelago ("Home" can read as an islet's name). Code: `UI/Sheets/ChartSheet.cs` (`PlaceName`, camp rows), `ChartData.cs` (`PlaceName`, `FoodWords`).
- 2026-10-04 — **The mill pauses while nothing can bake its flour (Kevin).** Every recipe that takes flour (bread, ship's biscuit, fish pie) wants a Kitchen II or more, so a mill beside a Kitchen I piled flour up (Kevin's camp: 583). **Now** a mill does not load a NEW flour batch while the camp's store holds **6 or more flour AND no recipe that takes flour can run here** (its station stands and `RecipeAvailable` allows it: fire, station level, tool) -- one test, `OutpostLedger.FlourHasUse` / `HoldOf`, read by the hold and by the "Flour waiting · Kitchen II bakes bread" alert alike. A batch already on the bench finishes. The miller stays posted (assignments are permanent) and waits at his bench as a station waiting on inputs does; nobody fetches wheat into the held bay (the worker, the runners' need and bay chores skip it). The spot, the station's stall line and the miller's line say **"Mill paused · nothing bakes flour yet"**; the camp list row reads "paused"; the flour alert stays the one chip and the fix-it pointer (the miller's own stall chip stands aside). It resumes by itself when any flour recipe becomes usable (a Kitchen II stands) or the store drops below 6 -- derived from state every step, nothing saved; held at `TryLoadSpot`, the one door every batch goes through, so the time-away catch-up obeys it. Self-test `FlourPauseSelfTest.Run()`; unplayed.
- 2026-10-05 — **Runners never walk to a station the store hut cannot reach.** Kevin's Day-853 camp: the fishing hut and pier stood outside the palisade, below the cliff, and runners booked there stood at the store front with no route until the trip timed out, again and again. Kevin chose: hauls skip a station with no walkable route from the store hut (re-checked whenever a wall, gate or building changes, so a breach or a new gate lets them back in at once); a hauler who finds no route for 5 s hands the trip back before pickup, goods staying booked where they are; the station says why ("Fishing hut · walled off from the store", or "no way there from the store" for a cliff), beside the "Walled off · needs a gate" chip, both opening the nearest wall piece. Assignments stay as they are.
- 2026-10-04 — **Kraken loot and the top-down camera.** Kevin: loot crates at a big haul's size (meat ~1.6 m, ink ~0.9 m) with no harpoon ring on them, the 🪝 button still targets them; the kraken surfacing astern and framing from far out stays as it is (not seen yet); and *"when sailing and changing the camera angle to looking straight down on the boat, I want it to zoom out a bit more, like the game I referenced"*: the sea camera backs off with the downward tilt (1.4x at `pitchMaxDeg`, FEEL-tunable).
- 2026-10-04 — **The kraken's swats cannot be cancelled.** Kevin: *"Swats can't be cancelled. The shots land where they land."* Removed the raised-arm skill shot: no hit cancels a windup (the interrupt call, the hit-flinch and the interrupt counter on `KrakenSwat` are gone), no lobbing, no padded capsule (`KrakenTuning.armHitRadius` 3 m -> 1.5 m, the arm's own thickness). Cannonballs keep their normal ballistics; a ball that physically hits a raised arm still costs it `armHitDamage` (0.35 of a body hit) and does nothing else; head hits as before. Every swat completes once its windup starts; steering out of the foam ring is the only answer. The one thing that still calls a windup off is the kraken itself leaving (`Kraken.Retreat` -> `KrakenSwat.CancelAll`: driven off, escaped, dismissed). `SweepCheck arm_hit` now asserts the swat is NOT cancelled. Kevin confirmed both follow-ups the same day: **the arm capsule stays at 1.5 m** (the arm's real thickness, no padding) and **the retreat call-off stays** (a beaten, sinking kraken never slams).
- 2026-10-04 — **The harpooner: a named hand mans the bow harpoon.** PLAN-harpoon §8 decision 7 (Kevin): *"A crew member mans the harpoon. The captain fires it, slower, if nobody is free."* Built as the harpoon's place in the shipboard priority list (rescue -> bail -> engaged guns / harpoon -> sails/oars), and only that: while the gun has work, a **spare hand** (not a gunner, free, nearest the bow) walks on deck to the mount's stand and mans it; else a **gunner whose gun faces the disengaged side** (out of a fight, any gunner), but only while the gunners left still cover every gun the engaged side(s) need (the gun shift's committed side, after its 3 s hold); else the **captain**, slower. A posted hand stays posted (no swapping for a nearer one); a borrowed gunner is handed back to the guns the moment the fight needs him. While posted, the gun shift treats him as away (his gun stands empty and may be covered) and never mans a gun from him. He works at his own work rate; a seasick harpooner shoots less steadily (accuracy 1 - 0.6 x sick, floor 0.3). Released (~1 s without work) he lingers ~3 s, then walks back to the post he came from (a gunner: to whatever gun the gun shift gives him). Overboard, knocked over the side by a kraken swat, ashore, hauling, in the jolly boat or bailing: dropped at once, his old post put back under him. **Bailing outranks the harpoon only when the buckets need him:** nobody bails on a dry bilge, and the bail order is idle spares first, then the harpooner, then the gunners (aftmost first) -- a borrowed gunner has no gun while posted but is never sent to the buckets as if he were an idle spare (`CrewRoster.BailOrder`). A refit that re-posts him sends him back to the bow. Player's ship only; runtime only (not saved). Code: `Ship/Harpoon/HarpoonCrewSource` (the gun's `CrewSource`, added with the gun by `PlayerHull`), rules `HarpoonCrewRules` (self-test `HarpoonCrewRules.SelfTest()`), `CrewAgent.HarpoonPosted`, `CannonBattery.HandsForFight` / `SideOfGun`. Unplayed.
- 2026-10-04 — **Storehouse merged into the store hut (Kevin).** *"merge them into the right building. Remove the building I built on the other side of the wall."* The store hut carries the runner progression: L1 2 runner posts; L2 4 posts + every runner's barrow +1 armful + jog 10% faster (price unchanged, 6 brick + 2 fine boards); **L3 (new)** 6 posts + barrow +2 armfuls + jog 20% faster, 14 brick + 8 fine boards (the old Storehouse L3 bill, provisional), fire II until fire III exists, standard level look until art. Perks from the highest-level store hut; copies add posts, not perks. The Storehouse is removed entirely (build list, plan, steps, caps, slots, sheets, goal chain). **One-time load repair** at Kevin's request (the one exception to buildings-never-move): each saved Storehouse comes down, its build + upgrade cost is refunded into that camp's store (25 timber + 4 stone; +8 brick + 4 fine boards at L2; +14 brick + 8 fine boards more at L3), its runners go to free store hut posts else Idle; nothing else moves; idempotent. Accepted gap (minor): a still-queued Storehouse blueprint is dropped with its carried goods refunded, but trees already felled on its plot are not put back on the camp's tree count (no saved plot anywhere had one). Supersedes the 2026-10-03 Storehouse entries. See "The store hut is runner progression" in the island loop section. Self-test `StorehouseMergeSelfTest.Run()`; unplayed.
- 2026-10-04 — **Farm sheet: show what is still ripe; a crop change never relabels a standing harvest.** Two bugs from the villager review, fixed together (`OutpostLedger.Food.cs`, `FarmSheet.cs`, `FarmPlotSelfTest`). (1) The plot tile read the crop's full yield on a part-harvested plot; it now reads `FarmPlot.RipeUnits` (`left` if set, else the yield) through one helper, `FarmPlot.StateLine`. Since 2026-10-03's infinite stacking the farmhand lifts the whole yield and `left` stays 0, so a fresh game cannot part-harvest; the field is kept only for saves from the few hours it was live. (2) `SetPlotCrop` on a ripe plot rewrote `crop` at once, so ripe wheat turned into ripe potatoes (goods relabelled). Conservative rule: the ripe crop keeps its identity and units; the pick is queued in `nextCrop` ("next: Potato") and applied by `FarmPlot.Harvested()` when the harvest is picked, planted next even with repeat off; re-picking the standing crop cancels it; Clear on a ripe plot = harvest, then bare. The replant toggle has its own door (`SetPlotRepeat`) so it no longer cancels a queued pick. Saved (`nextCrop`, default "" for old saves). Gate: `tools/selftest-outside-editor/run.sh SeaSick.World.FarmPlotSelfTest.Run` (14 gates). Unplayed.
- 2026-10-04 — **A station worker never leaves his stand to load or unload the bench.** Kevin: *"the people working at the stations ... leave their station with a product, walk around to the front, place it, and walk back ... They should be able to get the raw resource and place the finished product without leaving their station."* Cause: the books already put a finished batch on the rack, but the body then walked it to `Output_Dropoff`, the runners' mark at the front (the sawmill's out of its rear pad gate and round, ~8 m each way; kitchen and grain mill ~5 m). **Now** a finished batch is handed onto the rack from the stand (a turn and the set-down stoop toward the rack) and each new batch is taken off the bay the same way (a turn and the pick-up toward the bay); the rack and bay views show the goods. A rack holding the bench's own input (fine boards from boards) feeds the bay in the books, in place, with no trip. Runners still carry everything to and from the station at its front marks; with no runners the worker still fetches from the store or the field himself, and still carries a blocking rack home. Code: `CampWorker.BenchHandOff`, `OutpostLedger.OwnRackFeed`.
- 2026-10-04 — **Every stalled or locked station line names EVERY missing tool and input, most blocking first, each with where to get it.** Kevin (the home sawmill's alert read *"Sawmill · out of boards · pick boards here to make more (Yara)"*): *"it's set to fine boards but I don't have a saw blade"*. Fine boards need 2 boards AND a saw blade (tool, wear 0.05 an output), and the line stopped at the first gap it found. **Now** one pure builder (`Economy.MissingWords`, used by `OutpostLedger.MissingLine`) writes the line for every station: *"Sawmill · fine boards need a saw blade (make one at the forge) and boards (switch to Boards here) (Yara)"*. **Order:** a lock (fire / building level) first, then a missing tool, then an input nobody can supply now (worked out of the ground, its maker not built, won at sea), then an input that is just short, then a gatherable still standing (named, never a stall by itself). **Where to get it:** made at another station ("make one at the forge", "saw some at the sawmill", "build a forge to make one"), made at this same bench by switching recipe ("switch to Boards here") or on the building's other spot ("run Iron on the smelter"), "gather on the island" / "none left on this island", "grow it at the farm", "hunt for it". Some of a good in stock reads "more boards". **Everywhere one source:** the worker's reason (`StallReason`, so the alert chip, Problems list, hand sheet, workers list and building status chip), the spot's pause reason, and the recipe card's locked/short line (the card now lists the lock, the saw blade AND the inputs; the spot tile says "short of supplies" and leaves the sentence to the wrapping detail line). Wraps in full everywhere it is shown (`white-space: normal`), never cut. **Several spots:** a bench stalled on all its spots says the WORST spot's line (most blocking gap; the earlier spot on a tie) plus "· +N more" in the alert chip (its tap opens the station); the station sheet's stopped lines list every stalled spot, one per line, worst first. The legacy one-input path in `StallCause` goes through the same builder via a one-recipe stand-in (reachable by no plan today). **Worn tools (follow-up, same day):** Kevin's home store held a saw blade at whole 0, part 0.90 -- a usable blade with 90% left -- and every count floored it to 0, so the *display* said he had none while the books (and the work) were right. Display only: a part-worn tool (recipe tools, spears, the bow) shows as 1 with its life ("1 (90%)" in number cells, "Saw blade: 90% left." on the recipe card), appears in the Backpack and the store tiles, and the recipe tile no longer calls a worn blade missing. Ledger maths unchanged. Self-test: `MissingWordsSelfTest.Run()`. Unplayed.
- 2026-10-04 — **Home docking unloads cargo only; the repair stock stays aboard (Kevin).** The first 12 Timber in the hold (`HomeUnload.RepairStockTimber`, one full hull rebuild at `HullIntegrity.timberPerHullPoint`) is the ship's repair stock and is not ordered ashore at the home pier; every other kind, and timber above the stock, is ordered "all ashore" as before. A Backpack-sheet order can still carry the stock ashore. **The 12-timber stock is Kevin-confirmed (2026-10-04).** Self-test `HomeUnload.SelfTest()`; unplayed.
- 2026-10-04 — **Home docking unloads like any camp; the home bank (`banked`) is retired; one-time save repair.** Kevin (phone build 74116cd7): *"I came back with iron ore on my ship and it just vanished when I docked. It happened again now with stone."* Cause: since home became a player's camp (2026-09-29) the homecoming still ran the pre-camp path — `VoyageManager.CompleteVoyage` added the whole hold to a home-only number (`banked`, the save's top-level `banked`), drew crate piles on the beach (`Stockpile`) and emptied the hold, while the home camp's own store, which every sheet reads, got nothing; the camp's unloading was switched off at home (`ShipCargoSide.Present`, `AddLoot`, `CampLoading.RoomAboard` all refused `AtHome`). Nothing was destroyed, only misbooked. **Now:** (1) arriving at the home pier leaves the hold **aboard**; the home camp's hands (its runners when it has any) carry it ashore armful by armful into the home camp's store through the ordinary transfer path — a good counts on delivery (deliveries-on-arrival). The homecoming places one "all of it, ashore" order per kind aboard (stop or reverse it on the Backpack sheet like any order); loading at the home pier works like at any camp; (2) casting off **no longer empties the hold** (it used to clear it, harmlessly, because the hold had already been banked); (3) the yard (`ShipPrices`, `TargetLine`, the yard panel) and `VoyageManager.TryBuild` read and spend the **home camp's store** (`OutpostLedger.SpendableOf` / `Take`: store, racks, finished benches, never a bay); cargo still aboard at home does not pay until it is carried ashore. No home camp = no home pier = no yard (`AtHome` is false without one), so there is no fallback purse; (4) no crate piles are drawn at home any more (no ground piles); (5) **one-time repair on load** (`HomeBankRepair`, `SaveGame.Restore` step 5c2 — after the outposts and the home berth, before time away): an old save's `banked` rows are added to the home camp's store and cleared; idempotent; with no home camp (its pier gone) the bank is carried through saves untouched and nothing reads or spends it until a home camp exists (repaired then on the next load or homecoming). Kevin's a1 save: home store Timber 18 → 45, Stone 0 → 26, Ore 0 → 20. The ship's hold limits are unchanged. Self-test: `HomeBankRepair.SelfTest()`. Unplayed.
- 2026-10-04 — **Harpoon: fire only from one fixed button; markers are indicators.** Kevin, after his phone: "pressing the button to launch the harpoon is spotty at best. It's impossible to press it without it pressing on my ship. Also I'd like the harpoon button to be fixed on the screen, not above whatever it's targeting." The marker tap (which only re-picked the target and let the tap through to the Ship sheet) is gone; the button is bigger, fixed, always shown while the gun is fitted, and sealed against every other tap reader.
- 2026-10-04 — **The bow harpoon, phase 1 (hook & reel), approved and built.** Kevin: *"a harpoon gun on the front of the ship that is used to collect resources in the ocean"*. He decided: auto-target the nearest target in a 90° bow arc (tap a marker to switch, the barb leads itself, markers on targets); unlimited ammo for now; lines snap under held strain, then a 5 s reload; every ship has a basic bow harpoon from the start; a crew member mans it, and the captain fires it, slower, when nobody is free. Salvage clamps to the hold (a full hold keeps the load at the rail). Phase 1 = salvage only: land pull, raiders, the kraken and upgrades wait for his play verdict (`docs/PLAN-harpoon.md` §6, §9).
- 2026-10-03 — **Ore outcrop on every large island; one in two elsewhere (was one in three).** Kevin found a massive island with timber and stone and no iron. Rule (`TerrainWorldPopulator.WantsOreOutcrop`): the first non-home island built always has an outcrop, **every island with a mean shoreline radius >= 100 m (`LargeIslandRadius`, the top of the 60-110 m settlement band: roughly the largest third of the islands that get resources) always has one**, every other non-Ore island has one when `(HashCentre >> 8) % 6` is 0, 1 or 3 — one in two, and a superset of the old one in three (`% 3 == 0`), so no island a save already found ore on loses it. Still hash only, no `Random` draw from the world's stream, Ore-kind islands still skipped. A guaranteed island may not come up empty: if the usual scatter finds no ground, `BuildProps` retries on the same seeded stream with a wider, looser search, then a ring scan over any dry ground. Old saves gain outcrops by hash on load; props, stream and buildings are untouched, and a camp that gains one books it through `Outpost.EnsureOreStock` (called from `CatchUp`). `TerrainWorldPopulator.OreReport()` lists every island with its radius, large flag and outcrop. Unplayed.
- 2026-10-03 — **Landing party: berries, and tile counts that match the trip.** Kevin: a massive island read 79 timber, 169 stone, goats and boar, but no berries or other raw food. Wild berry bushes (`SceneryCrops`, `BedKind.Berry`) are now a party Food source like trees and rocks: counted, tiled ("berries"), picked (one Food a bush, the ledger loses the bush's 0.5 yield), stripped in the mesh, booked by name (`OutpostLedger.takenBeds` + regrow day, 20 days = `RegrowPerDay(Food)`), saved with the ledger; held out of the camp field's units so the camp's count and the party's take share one ledger. The tile and "All N" now read one list (`IslandInventory.Sources`) with one walk answer per source, so they promise only what the party can fetch; the old nearest-48-trees / 60-rocks node caps are a standing budget topped up as nodes are used (`GatherParty.StandMore`). **Decided (Kevin, 2026-10-03): a landing party carries one Food a bush** (the camp's books still drop the bush's 0.5 yield); unplayed.
- 2026-10-03 — **Kevin: gunners walk to the engaged side.** A gun is worked by whoever is STANDING at it, not by the hand it was built for. **Engaged side** = the side of the locked target (`CombatLock` → `CannonBattery.AutoFireTarget`), else of the nearest hostile within the combat row's reach (1.5× the guns' flat range, at least 60 m). Gunners whose gun faces away **walk** (no snap; deck gait through `CrewAgent.WalkTo`, so the coaster's deck graph routes them, ~6 s across) to **unmanned** guns on that side, nearest along the deck first; a gun fires only once someone has arrived at it (`Relocating` is not `Available`). **Hysteresis:** the side (or "no fight") must hold **3 s** before anybody moves, so a kraken flipping sides or a raider crossing the bow does not march the crew back and forth. **Enemies both sides:** the locked side fills first; spare gunners man the other side. **After the fight** (no lock, nobody in reach, the same 3 s) everyone walks back to his **home gun**. A hand's **rail spot** (heaving) and **bucket spot** follow the gun he is at, and the **bail order** is now by the gun he is at (spares first, then aftmost guns first) instead of a fixed berth table. Hands overboard, ashore, hauling or in the jolly boat drop out of the plan; their gun stands empty and may be covered; when they come back they never bump whoever covered. A full crew (one hand per gun) moves nobody — the walk only happens when the ship has fewer gunners than guns. Authored-fleet hulls (one hand per port/stbd pair) keep their pairing: each hand walks to his pair's gun on the engaged side. Runtime only: on load everyone starts at his home gun. Player ship only (raiders work their own guns). Rules: `Ship/GunCrewShift.cs` (pure, `GunCrewShift.SelfTest()`); deck side: `CannonBattery.RebalanceCrews`. **Not built (later):** the full shipboard priority list (rescue → bail → engaged guns → sails/oars).
- 2026-10-03 — **Sailing controls = DREDGE.** Kevin: *"I don't like how the movement and camera work when sailing. I want you to do exactly how DREDGE does their movement input and camera input"*, and *"a little more punch in the boost"*. Decided: the stick is **boat-relative** (up/down speed ∝ push, left/right turn); **portrait two-zone** (boat stick lower 55 %, camera stick upper 45 %, one thumb is enough); **no pinch / wheel zoom at sea** (the island keeps its pinch); a **⚡ boost button** (Shift on desktop), **free until fuel exists**; **R stays rowing**, recenter goes on C / middle mouse / double-tap. Releasing the stick stops the engine; nothing latches. Built in steps 1-2 (`Ship/SeaStick.cs`, `Ship/HelmInput.cs`, `CameraRig/SeaCameraInput.cs`, Settings → CAMERA) and step 3 (boost punch: FOV kick + shake + haptic + surge, FEEL → BoostTuning; dead-code sweep). **Supersedes:** the 2026-09-22 one-wheel / telegraph / burn-rim stick, the 2026-09-23 floating heading-autopilot stick, tap-to-stop, the Oars / Ease toggles, the sea pinch zoom, and "No manual camera". The 2026-08-19 "Combat camera: widen, never rotate" below is about *framing* and still holds, but its premise (the camera is the steering frame) no longer applies: steering ignores the camera. Plan and removal map: `docs/PLAN-dredge-controls.md`. Numbers PROVISIONAL, tuned on Kevin's phone.
- 2026-10-03 — **Kevin: the landing party reaches the whole island, nothing moves, far = longer trip.** The 80 m `GatherParty.Reach` cap is gone. A source is offered and worked when a hand can walk to it: (1) a straight walkable line from the landing (`LineWalkable`, as before, now uncapped), else (2) a route over the island's own walk grid — `CampPath`, the camps' A* map, which a surveyed camp-less island has too — answered for every source at once by ONE Dijkstra flood from the landing (`CampPath.FloodFrom`, cached until the landing moves; cost unmeasured on the phone, same order as the grid's own build); else not at all. Hands walk the route's corners out and retrace them home (`CrewAgent.partyRoute`); they pick the source with the shortest WALK from the landing. Nothing is relocated for the party: ore that `PlaceCampStone` rings round the surveyed clearing (inland) is now listed and gatherable where it stands; Ore/Stone islands' seams stay put. Trees (48 nearest) and party rocks (60 nearest) are stood up over the whole island, nearest first. Sheet: a tile whose nearest source is past 80 m shows **"far · m:ss"** (out and back at shore pace + one armful); an unreachable kind reads "cut off". Hunting never had the cap (a hunter walks straight at the nearest beast; unchanged). Compile-checked; unplayed.
- 2026-10-03 — **Kevin: "remove the fog of war."** Every island is fully visible, always; supersedes the 2026-09-30 "Island fog" entry. **Removed:** the reveal grid (`IslandFog`), its world cloud (`IslandFogView`, `Art/Shaders/World/IslandFog.shader`, the keepalive material), the chart's cloud bands, the shore strip opened on anchoring, the "% explored" pill and the report's "Found …" / "% explored" lines. **No longer gated:** a landing party gathers any source and hunts any animal on the island; the sheet's tiles and chips and the chart's find diamonds / herd dots (landed camp-less islands) show everything there (`World/IslandInventory.cs` = the fog's old counters without the reveal test); the "Explore first" buttons are plain text; the sheet opens on Gather. **Saves:** `SaveData.fog` is deleted; `JsonUtility` skips the old `"fog"` key, so saves from 2026-09-30..10-03 load clean (no error, no version bump) and new saves stop writing it. **Explore** only ever opened ground (nothing spawned or unlocked; finds are collected by sailing past), so it is now a 120 s walk with the boar risk and no reward, (removed the same day at Kevin's word, see "Explore removed" above). The sea HUD's place line off a camp-less island is now just the island's name (was "Island_7 · 20%"); "settled" is `Outpost.IsSettled`. `IslandFog.cs` is deleted outright. Not touched: the chart's island discovery (`Discovery`, unseen / glimpsed / landed islands), a separate system. Compile-checked; unplayed.
- 2026-10-03 — **Kevin: Explore removed; Gather and Hunt stay.** *"remove the Explore card."* With the fog gone (entry below) Explore opened nothing, spawned nothing and unlocked nothing: a 120 s walk with a boar-bite roll and the report "Nothing new found". **Deleted:** the card and its page (ON THIS ISLAND chips, send button, progress page, compass glyph), `GatherParty.Order.Explore`, `SendExplore`, the stop picker and breadcrumb trail home, the boar-encounter roll on a walk (`BoarDangerMetres`, `HurtChanceUnarmed/Armed`) and the explore dials (`ExploreSeconds/Step/Reach`), the report's "Nothing new found" and "drove off a boar" lines. **Kept (Gather/Hunt use them):** the hurt-never-dead hand (a hunt kill still rolls `LifeTuning.HuntAccidentChance`; he carries the carcass home and rests aboard `HurtRestSeconds`, logged Downed), recall, the party's report toast, `IslandInventory`. The sheet opens on Gather with two cards. **Saves:** a landing party is never saved (the save writes the crew by name and the hold, so a party ashore loads as recalled, every hand aboard) and nothing saved names an order, so old saves load clean with no migration. Compile-checked only; unplayed.
- 2026-10-03 — *(Superseded 2026-10-04: the Storehouse is merged into the store hut.)* **Storehouse = runner progression (Kevin).** Stores are unlimited, so the Storehouse's +20-of-each is retired; each level now adds runner posts on top of the store huts' (L1 2, L2 4, L3 6) and a camp-wide perk for every runner from the highest-level Storehouse (L1 barrow +1 armful; L2 + jog 10% faster; L3 barrow +2 armfuls, jog 20% faster). L3 is new, needs fire II (Kevin 2026-10-03: fire III does not exist yet), costs 14 brick + 8 fine boards (provisional). Kevin approved the placeholder look at camps until art exists. The store hut's dead +10 store bonus went too (its level 2 is 4 runners). The Storehouse, home-only until now, joined the camp build list with a placeholder model. See "The Storehouse is runner progression" in the island loop section.
- 2026-10-03 — **Storage: Kevin approved the storage-slot containers preview, then replaced the capacity rule with infinite stacking — the containers are visual.** (`art-staging/storage-slots-preview/SPEC.md`.) Stored goods are shown in authored container slots (one bundle of one resource, fill steps; seven shared families; store hut L1 4/3/3/6/6/4/4 slots, fire cache 2/1/1/2/2/1/1) and `CampPiles`' store drawing is retired (ring by the fire, hut-side stacks, kitchen fish pile and crate); dropped loads and deck cargo stay. Replaces the uniform `ceilingPer` of each resource (fire 10 + hut 20 + L2 10). **Kevin's decisions the same day:** (1) *"remove storage limits. infinite stacking is allowed"* — **island** stores never refuse goods; every store-full gate is gone (gatherers/hunters resting, the farm's part harvest, runners' dropped-load-for-no-room, rack jams, the island side of ship transfers, the food draft's "fed with no room left" end — its 6-day end stays —, the "Store full" alert, home banking's cap); slots fill at their bundle sizes and stay shown full past them while the sheets show the true count; (2) *"the ship will not have infinite storage"* — hold and cargo limits unchanged; (3) bundles: **sacks 12, dish trays 12, ship's biscuit 12 a tray**, pegs 4; (4) **no level-2 slots** — a store hut's level 2 is about its runners (4 instead of 2); the store hut stays the runners' home and workplace and the visible store; (5) an **idle** hand's store top-up stops once that family's visible slots are full, then he rests (option b); assigned workers go on without limit; (6) art: tarp moved back to an open stall front, fire groundsheet 3.2 × 2.3 m, fill steps shipped as a shared kit (`StorageFillKit`) instantiated into empty anchors instead of baked into each building (~163k → ≤28k tris a hut). Provisional, for Kevin: the storehouse shown as twice a store hut *(moot 2026-10-04: no Storehouse)*. The store is invisible in the world until the container FBXs are imported. Code: `StorageSlots`, `OutpostLedger.RoomFor`/`SlotCapacityOf`/`SlotRoomFor`, `Outpost.StoreSlotsNow`, `StorageSlotView`, `CampPiles` (now only `DrawPile`/`BuildUnit`/`StoreBuildingOf`). Compile-checked only; unplayed.
- 2026-10-03 — **Villager review group 4: a Problems list, runners that never forget a waiting bay, part loads into a short bay.** (1) The alert strip's "+N" chip is now **"N problems"** and opens a short **Problems** sheet listing every `CampAlerts` alert (the same facts as Camp › NEEDS YOU), with three new station lines: a station with a recipe chosen and nobody on it (*"Kitchen · no cook"*), a bench that has waited on the runners for over 0.3 work-day (*"Sawmill · waiting on the runners for logs"*, tap = the store hut, where runners are added), and a stuck station worker now reads station first (*"Sawmill · needs logs (Yara)"*, tap = the station). (2) **Aging:** within each rung of the runners' ladder (idle benches, blocking racks, pre-stocking, full racks) the request waiting longest wins, in steps of 0.1 work-day; equal waits go nearest first; rung order unchanged; not saved (a reload restarts the clocks). Blueprints were already oldest first. (3) **Part loads:** a bay short of one batch already took any amount from the store or a rack; now a full rack load it cannot hold whole is tipped in as far as it fits (the rest stays on the rack) instead of all going to the store. Pre-stocking keeps the full-load rule. Code: `OutpostLedger.Runners.cs` (`AgeHaulRequests`, `RunnersSlowFor`), `OutpostLedger.Stations.cs` (`OfferAged`, `RackDest`), `UI/Sheets/ProblemsSheet.cs`, `AlertStrip.cs`, `CampAlerts.cs`. **Kevin approved 2026-10-03:** the "N problems" wording, the ~18 s (0.1 work-day) aging step and "waiting on the runners" after ~0.3 work-day. Unplayed; not screenshot-checked.
- 2026-10-03 — **Villager rule clean-up (review group 3): honest crew words, one food draft, no silent loss of a post.** (1) **"Idle" is retired as a crew word**; it meant three things. Now: **Reserve** — the player stood the hand down (`Outpost.OrderIdle`); the idle ladder, the food draft and the chip leave him be. **No job** — no order (or a gather order with nothing picked) and nothing the idle ladder can give him; the only hands the top-bar chip ("2 no job"), the "hands with no job" alert and the Workers subtitle count. **Builder — waiting for stone** — a builder with no plot to work while a site still lacks a material (named; "a plot" when every site has its materials and full crew); a held-up builder, not an unassigned one, and his reason line names the shortfall or the load on its way. (2) **One food draft** (supersedes the separate hunger draft and food emergency of 2026-09-28 / 2026-10-02): under **3 days** of food, free hands (no job, or the idle ladder's builders — never a player-sent builder, the reserve, a runner or anyone on other work) fill each Farm copy with a crop, then each Kitchen copy with something to cook, then hunt (else forage) as the fallback while nobody farms or under a day of food, up to a hand in four on gathering. One banner (*"Food low — Pip farming, Gale foraging"*, Undo all), one end: at **6 days**, or fed with nothing the posts make left room in the store — every drafted hand goes back to no job, where the idle ladder re-enlists him at once (the draft only ever takes no-job hands and the ladder's builders, so that is the job he had). The draft's cook takes an **empty** kitchen spot first; a player's dish it has to replace is remembered and **restored** when the draft ends or its cook is sent back. The player's "not him" (Undo) is **saved** and lasts until that draft ends (was ~3 real minutes, unsaved). Any player order clears a hand's draft flags, so the draft's end never undoes a player's order. (3) **One worker per station never silently erases a saved job:** a pinned hand keeps his copy over a dealt one; an extra moves to another copy of the same building with room; only with every copy full is he sent to no job, and the alert strip names him (*"Finch lost the sawmill post · one worker per station"*, saved until tapped or he is posted again). Code: `OutpostLedger.Status.cs` (`NoJobWord`, `BuilderWaitPrefix`), `OutpostLedger.FoodDraft.cs`, `OutpostLedger.Copies.cs` (`EnforceStationCaps`, `postsLost`). **Kevin confirmed 2026-10-03:** the veto lasting until that draft ends, the hunt/forage fallback rule, Reserve hands left off the "no job" chip, and the Workers button's "Assign N free hands". Unplayed.
- 2026-10-03 — **The kraken: a deep-water monster that fights back, driven off rather than killed.** Kevin approved the design (his own Meshy octopus, 7 arms, ~65 m across, rigged and prefabbed at 3.5×; severity chosen by him: three hits cripple, never sink). Deep water only and rarer near home, always telegraphed (shadow, edge chevron, toast, vibration, ~10 s), one arm swats at a ring of foam laid where the ship is heading, with run / fight (hit the raised arm to cancel its swat) / drive-off answers and kraken meat plus a rare kraken ink as loot. No new phone buttons; numbers live in a FeelLab class. Full spec in §6 "The Kraken (deep-water monster)"; the 2026-08-19 "Still open" on monsters dealing no damage now points there. Build step 1 (surfaces, harmless) in progress; steps 2-4 not built.
- 2026-10-02 — **Assignments are permanent (Age-of-Empires style): mood and hunger never stop work.** Kevin: *"we need to rework the villagers quitting or being too unhappy to work. the micro management is too much at the moment. see it as age of empires style. you assign someone somewhere, thats what they do. i cant try to keep track of every person at all times to figure out what theyre doing. that way ill never get to sails anywhere."* Approved: *"ok no quiting changes."* (1) **No pouting, ever** — not from low mood and not from grief after a death (the earlier same-day grief pout is retired too); `OutpostLedger.PoutStep` only ends a pout an old save left running and drops any grief still owed, `ForcePout` does nothing. (2) **Hunger barely slows work:** `StarvingWorkFloor` 0.35 → **0.8** (a starving hand works at four-fifths pace). (3) **No food drafts of the player's people:** the food emergency (`FeedFirst`, `FreeFoodHand`) still puts *idle* hands and builders the idle ladder enlisted on food, but never a builder the player sent (`OutpostHand.playerBuild`, set by `Outpost.OrderBuild`) — nor, as before, anyone on a gather/work order or held in reserve. Instead the food alert names the fix: *"Out of food · assign a gatherer or cook"* / *"Food low · assign a gatherer or cook"*. Mood stays a visible stat (top bar, Mood sheet, angry-hands alert) and still drifts with supper. Old saves: a saved pout/grief clears on the first tick; `playerBuild` reads false, so builders in an old save stay draftable until re-ordered.
- 2026-09-30 — **The landing party replaces the gather party: Explore, Gather, Hunt on one half-height sheet.** Kevin: *"this whole interaction needs to be re worked."* See §6 Island UI structure, "The landing party". Old `GatherPartySheet` (own panel, own USS) deleted; the trip logic stays in `Ship/GatherParty.cs`. Cannon fire chips hide with no enemy in range; the IMGUI Ledger/Menu chips stand down off a fresh island (☰ on the sheet instead).
- 2026-09-30 — **The hold shows on deck as barrels, sacks and crates (Astra ship cargo, Kevin approved 2026-09-29).** The coaster's hold was invisible (the hands set each unit down at the storage hatch). `ShipCargoDisplay` stands a few chunky props in fixed deck sockets laid per hull section (bulwark pairs clear of the helm, funnel, ladders, hatch and gun bays; the base boat has 6: a pair by the bow hatch, a pair forward of the helm, one starboard beside it (the funnel takes port), one on the prow's centreline). Props shown = ceil(held / the marked line × sockets), so an empty hold is a bare deck and a full one fills every socket; **deck cargo** past the line stacks a smaller second prop, up to every socket doubled at the physical limit. Container by `ResDefs` category: Food in barrels; grain (wheat, flour) and Raw bulk (timber, stone, ore, hide) in sacks; Material, Gear and everything made in crates. All on the shared GameColor toon material; no colliders — the sockets are reserved in the crew's walk graph instead. Unplayed.
- 2026-09-30 — **SUPERSEDED 2026-10-03 (the fog of war is removed; see that entry).** **Island fog: unsettled islands start under cloud; explorers open it.** Kevin's approved mockups "7 · Landing party: Explore (fog)" / "7b · Gather". Every island without a camp (`Outpost.HasCamp`) or any standing building is fogged; settled islands (and home) are always clear, and making camp fades the whole cover away over a second. **Grid** (`World/IslandFog.cs`): one bit per ~5 m cell over the island's own component of the populator's flood-fill mask (cells grow past 5 m only above 96 a side, i.e. a 250 m landmark gets ~6 m cells); a cell is land when the ground is above 0.5 m and the mask says the island owns it, and `Revealed01` is the share of land cells open. **Opening:** anchoring opens the landing strip (`RevealShoreNear`: beach within 40 m of the nearest land to the ship, higher ground only within 18 m; `IslandFogView` calls it on the anchor's Anchored/Ashore transition, idempotent), explorers call `RevealAround(pos, ~25 m)` as they walk. What is open stays open and is **saved** per island (`SaveData.fog`, rows keyed by island centre with name fallback, the bit array as base64; an optional field, no version bump: an old save loads with every camp-less island fogged). **Found** = what stands in open ground: harvest nodes (kit nodes by their units, scenery trees a log each, loose rocks by size, nothing already taken by name), live animals (`Animal`; there is no herd type) and uncollected `IslandFind`s. **Look** (`World/IslandFogView.cs`, `Art/Shaders/World/IslandFog.shader`): one soft cloud mesh per fogged island within 1.1 km of the camera, draped 15 m over the highest ground near each vertex (above the canopy) and sloping down to the water at the coast so the sea sees a cloud bank, not a ceiling; alpha from the grid as an R8 texture, five bilinear taps plus slow drifting billow noise, lit by the sun and sky so dusk and night carry through. The land under it reads dimmed and greyed, trees and props faint shapes at most. Opened cells fade out over ~1 s; while a building is being sited on the anchored island the cover thins to a veil so the ground can be chosen. Cost: one transparent draw per fogged island in view, a byte step over the changed cells and one small texture upload while a reveal fades, no per-frame allocations. **Chart:** a landed, unsettled island is clouded where unexplored (round-capped cloud bands over its fill, cached per fog change); finds (ember diamonds) and animals (dots) found by the party show on the chart only — no icons over world objects. **Known gap:** a shelter-only island's find (spotted from the sea) sits under the cloud too until explored. Unplayed.
- 2026-09-30 — **Castaways at sea: people found floating in the water.** Kevin: *"Please add so people can be found floating in the water."* A NEW person (never your own crew) clings to a floating board (Astra's lashed bundle or long broken board) and waves, half in the water, as Astra's deckhand figure (`Ship/SeaLife/Castaway.cs`). About one per 10 min of live sailing (±25 %), one at a time, 110–200 m ahead within 60° of the bow, never near land (the 60 m shore margin flotsam uses), over less than 4 m of water or on a reef; not while paused, away, anchored or in a fight (raid party ashore, an enemy raiding/chasing, anything hostile within 300 m); own RNG stream off the world seed (`Voyage/CastawaySpawner.cs`, knobs in `RecruitTuning`). The edge arrow and tap-to-steer from man overboard work on them. Within 12 m of the hull's side under 3 m/s, a big bottom **Pull <name> aboard** button draws them in on a line and boards them through the camp-born boarding path (never refused for berths), named from the new-hand name pool; ignored, they drift off after 4 min with no death and no penalty; nothing is saved while they are in the water. Aboard they are saved as crew. Anchored at any camp, they go ashore and join as an ordinary hand through `Outpost.Station`'s path (no ship's rations; beds are the camp's usual alert), and their story gains *"Pulled from the sea off <island> by <crew member>."* Unplayed.
- 2026-09-30 — **Crew and cargo go overboard from hits, not from sailing.** Kevin, after a phone playtest: *"crew and cargo fall off waaaay too easily ... It should be collisions or getting hit by enemies."* Roughness, ordinary heel and wave slams no longer drain grip or lashing (only a near-capsize, heel past 45° sustained, still can); smoothness still drives seasickness. A hard collision (rocks, reef, shore, another hull) above 4.5 m/s closing speed, or an enemy cannonball hit, now rolls a chance to throw a hand and/or knock a crate loose, scaled with impact strength (`Ship/Overboard/HitOverboard.cs`, knobs in `OverboardTuning`); bulwarks and safety lines still cut the crew chance. The scripted first man overboard is now the first such hit, not a 90 s timer.
- 2026-09-29 — **No pre-made home; you start at sea and make a camp your home.** Kevin: *"remove the 'home' island. the intended start for this game is that you are on the base version of the ship (nose and butt of ship, no middle section and only one floor). you have 4 crew and the captain. you choose yourself which island to settle and once youve built a campfire and a pier/ dock you can make it into your home island."* The authored home island is off (`homeIsle` 0) and no island is built as home. A new game starts in open water near the world origin facing an island 80–400 m off. Home is whichever pier the player names: **"Make this island home"** on the ship sheet appears only when tied up at a pier on an island whose camp has its campfire, and home can move later by the same rule. `Island.IsHome`, `Outpost.Home`, `Stockpile.Instance` and `Settlement.Home` all follow `Dock.Home`; with no home the voyage loop idles and the Home tab, "home N m" and the compass home mark are hidden. Also: no wild wheat fields (`farmedShare` 0; berries stay the forage, farms still grow wheat) and no bare-earth paint under trees. **Still to do:** the base starting ship (low bow + low stern, no middle, one deck, one gun pair on the bow) and 4 crew + the helmsman as captain, waiting on the other session's uncommitted ship files. Fresh save needed. Unplayed.
- 2026-09-29 — **Astra's Island_2 nature kit and rules on every island.** Kevin, after the resize removed Island_2: *"i want that rule / those assets on all islands. making sure assets dont float"* and *"small island with mostly sand should have palmtrees. grass grows more closer to tree trunks"*. `IslandNatureProfile` (`allIslands`) now dresses every island: Astra's species by height/slope/grove, groups at trunk and rock bases, shore and cliff accents, resource props re-dressed (a palm island's timber is palms). The painted ground (moss under canopy, earth at roots) was one global texture, so one island; it is now a slice per island in `NatureGroundAtlas` plus a world index map the terrain shader reads (~18 MB GPU for ~70 islands). **Palm islands (new rule, not in Astra's notes):** `Palmy` = max(small AND mostly sand, the old latitude rule); a palm island's low ground and beach go to palms, and any tree the scatter stands on sand is a palm; elsewhere palms stay in Astra's coastal patches.
