Grounding is unchanged `NatureGrounding` (feet snapped to the rendered terrain mesh); measured max foot gap −4.5 cm (below ground) on every island checked; island finds now use it too. **Home keeps its authored `HomeIslandDressing`** (not changed without Kevin's say). Wheat fields are still the old kit's crop mats (the kit has no crop asset). The game's terrain settings are `Materials/GraphicArt/TerrainSettings.asset`, not `Settings/Terrain/` — the resize values are now in the right one. Unplayed; phone cost unmeasured.
- 2026-09-29 — **Island sizes: small near home, landmarks far out, a grain per region, a find on every small island.** Kevin asked what size range suits an idle + exploration phone game; the answer was about 60–110 m radius for settlements (the docked view holds ~165 m of ground), 40–70 m outposts, rare 150–250 m landmarks, and nothing reaching much past ~250–300 m. He said *"yes, do all three"* and asked whether islands could stretch in more than one direction. (1) **Size grows with distance:** inside `nearIslandRadius` (900 m) the island mask runs at `nearIslandScale` (1.6×) its frequency, blending to full size by `farIslandRadius` (2200 m). (2) **Regional grain:** the stretch is read along three axes 60° apart and a slow selector field (`grainRegionFrequency` 1/2600) picks one per region, so neighbouring islands share a grain and the next group runs another way; islands on a border come out bent. Both are blends of whole fields, variance-renormalised so `landRatio` still holds (`TerrainHeight.MaskNoiseBase`); a per-point rotation would shred the field at range. `maskStretch` 2 → 1.7, `maskFrequency` 1/700 → 1/800. Measured over 4 seeds (flood-fill census, same scan as the populator): near-home islands 9.5 → 16.5 per world, none ≥140 m radius (was 2), median 72 → 54 m; the ≥140 m islands now sit mostly past 2 km; reach p90 299 → 227 m, >300 m 6.5 → 3.2 per world. Height sample cost +20 %. (3) **Island finds** (`World/IslandFind`): every shelter-only island (mean radius < 60 m) gets one find on a beach: a washed-up **cache** (~65 %, 3–8 units of the ring's resource into the hold) or a lookout **cairn** (glimpses the two nearest unseen islands on the chart, banner names the direction). Sail within 35 m to collect; taken finds saved as `takenFinds`. Stand-in art. **The whole archipelago changed shape except home — camps on other islands in an old save do not survive it.** Unplayed.
- 2026-09-28 — **Raiders land where the walk in is shortest.** Kevin: *"Raiders should take the shortest reasonable route in. Right now they walk around half the island. If that's where they wanted to walk to, that's where the ship should land close to."* The landing used to be the beach nearest the fire BY SEA. Now `Outpost.BestLanding` scores 36 shore points round the island by the raiders' walk to the camp's store (a real route, or — when a wall is in the way — the walk to the nearest breachable wall + `RaidFightTuning.breachCostMetres` 40 m) and the raider ship lands at the cheapest; each raider ashore takes the cheaper of the open route and breaching (replacing the ">2× + 30 m" rule). One log line per raid gives the bearing and cost. Editor: Island_2 raid landed at 20°, 123 m, and the party reached the camp in under a minute.
- 2026-09-28 — **Grilled meat at the kitchen.** Kevin: *"Grilled meat needs to be added to the kitchen recipies. I have 30 meat and my village is starving."* Meat only went into hunter's stew (Kitchen III, fire II), so a hunting camp could starve on a full meat pile. New Kitchen I recipe **grilled meat**: 1 meat → 1 grilled meat, 20 s, fill 0.75 (between grilled fish 0.6 and roast carrots 1.0). `RecipeGraph.Validate` OK.
- 2026-09-28 — **Kevin's playtest fixes: graves, stuck villagers, raider detours.** Kevin: *"i cant place the gravestones, it just shows up red wherever i press"* — a grave lost at sea had no camp and was tied to the first watched camp (the empty home island) while he stood at Island_2; now a sea grave goes to the watched camp with people nearest the ship, follows a tap onto another camp's island, the bar says why a spot is refused, and it waits out a raid. Kevin: *"raiders choose to run all the way left around the island … several villagers get stuck … they should never get stuck"* — the new alarm/defence/evening walks demanded a closer arrival than the walker gives (fixed: the walker's own arrival counts), the drag was a straight line through walls (now routed), a walled-off hand waited forever (drops the errand after 3 asks), raiders "stole" at a cliff foot when no route was found and never breached when a round-the-island detour existed (now breach when the route is over 2× the straight line + 30 m), defenders re-planned raider routes every frame (cached), and the row said "hungry (0% pace)" for anyone busy or asleep. LIFE dev button moved right of FEEL (it covered the ☰ button).
- 2026-09-28 — **Weather you can see coming: squalls.** Kevin's pick (*"i like 4, 5"*). While sailing, about every 4 min a squall is born 700–1100 m off the bow and drifts across your path at 3 m/s (radius 260 m, lives 5 min, never over a camp): a dark cloud bank with a rain curtain, readable from a kilometre (`Ocean/Weather/SquallDirector`, `SquallVisuals` — 6 static quads/cylinder, no particles, no extra ocean samples). Banner *"A squall to the NW, heading this way"* plus an edge arrow while it is off screen; *"In the squall — ease her down"* inside; *"Through the squall"* out. Inside, the sea rises toward severity 0.85 through a new `SeaStateController.SetExternalBoost` (no squall = unchanged), so gusts, sky, grip, lashing and swimmer timers all follow; the boost also shrinks with speed, so riding it out slow is genuinely safer. Knobs in `SquallTuning`.
- 2026-09-28 — **Villagers with a day.** Kevin's pick from the "more alive" list. `CampLifeTuning` (World/Life), `CampWorker.Routine.cs`, `VillagerChatter.cs`. **Schedule (current, 2026-10-02):** a **480 s** sky day; **supper 21:00** (the bell: the camp eats at the fire, see §6 Food), **sleep 23:00**, **wake 01:00**. Evening: hands finish the leg they are on, then stand in a loose ring round the fire, eat their supper, and sway ("sing") when the camp was fed together; night: asleep in a hut with a free bed (2 per hut, nearest first, body hidden) or lying by the fire. **Output-neutral:** no work in the off-hours, watched or not (the catch-up walks each quantum at its own hour), and awake work ×24/20 (`CampLifeTuning.AwakeWorkScale`, derived from the hours), so a day's totals are what they were. A player order given at night breaks the routine for that hand until morning. **Speech lines** above heads (one at a time per camp, every ~12 s): pouting, hungry, a rescue they shared, a friend in the graveyard, going overboard, evening reminiscence, the job; defenders shout, hiders stay silent. LIFE panel: skip to evening/night/morning, *Say a line now*.
- 2026-09-28 — **Things to find at sea.** Kevin picked it from the "more alive" list (*"go ahead with 1 and 2 but i like 4, 5"*). `Ship/SeaLife/` (knobs in `SeaLifeTuning`), all only while sailing live and never within 60 m of a shore: **flotsam** every ~150 s (at most 2), 150–300 m ahead, 2–5 units of a common good — sail over it like a swimmer and it's in the hold; **message in a bottle** (1 in 5 spawns) marks the nearest unseen island as glimpsed on the chart; **fish shoals** — gulls over a dark patch; within 25 m under 3 m/s the crew fish +1 food every 4 s up to 6; **dolphins** at the bow after 20 s of fast (>4 m/s) smooth (roughness <0.2) sailing, easing sickness ×1.5 while they stay — pillar #1's reward. All get the ring, edge arrow and tap-to-steer; shoals are not "boardable". Primitive stand-in visuals.
- 2026-09-28 — **Man overboard pickup: sail over them.** Kevin: *"i cant pick a guy up whose landed in the water. it should be enough that i sail over him and a quick 2 second timer goes off and he climbs aboard."* The Throw line button is gone: any swimmer or floating crate within `OverboardTuning.boardReachMetres` (5 m) of the hull's side, or under her, for `boardSeconds` (2 s) comes aboard, with a filling *"Anna is climbing aboard"* bar bottom-centre; drifting out of reach restarts it. No speed gate. The scramble net halves the time, the lifebuoy rack widens the reach by 3 m; tap-to-steer, ring and edge arrow unchanged; the jolly boat still rows out on its own.
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 12: all clear and raids that grow.** When a raid ends (*"All clear."* on the banner): hiders come out, every hand holding a store spear **walks it back** and it is booked at the store (if the camp stops being watched mid-walk it is booked at once), hunters keep theirs, the downed are dragged to huts and dropped loads and raider loot are carried home by the ordinary phase-2 paths, then everyone returns to their job. **Spear wear:** each raider killed wears the defender's spear by 1/4 (stone) or 1/12 (iron) — the hunting numbers; a worn-through spear **breaks** (banner, `SpearBroke` life event, the hand hides if the raid is still on); a worn spear returns as a fraction in the store's whole+part pool, like hunting. **Scaling:** party size = 3 + one per 4 hands + at most one more per 200 stored units (the smoke run drew 7 raiders against 5 hands at one per 40), capped at 7, computed only when a raid lands; loot cap scales with it up to 16. Every hand still on the roster logs *lived through a raid* however it ended (a sunk ship too).
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 11: the Fight / Hide-all switch, banner counts, row reasons.** During a raid the banner's second line reads *"4 defending · 3 hiding · 1 spear left"* (`Combat/RaidBanner.cs`, from `RaidAlarm.Counts`). A big bottom button, ashore too, toggles **Hide everyone** (all but tower lookouts into the huts, spear or not; a raider fighting a hand who hides goes back to stealing) and **Send the armed out** (only hands holding a spear go back out); **H** on desktop. Every hand has a raid reason on his row (*fetching a spear*, *hiding (ordered)*, *hiding, no spear*, *crouching, no spear*, *defending, iron spear*, *on the tower*, *down*…). After a raid where hands hid for want of a spear, the camp pins a goal **Forge spears before the next raid** (met when the store holds that many spears, up to 4; `PinnedGoal.Stock`, `GoalChain.SpearStock`). Astra: the land RaidSheet should carry the counts and the switch.
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 10: the alarm, arming and hiding.** When a raid party lands (`RaidParty.Begin` → `Combat/RaidAlarm.Begin`) every hand drops his load where he stands; tower lookouts stay posted; downed/recovering/dragging hands carry on. Hunters out with a spear go straight to the fight. The spears in the store are real: the hands nearest the store (one per spear, iron first) walk there and take one at arrival (gone → they hide); each defender's own spear sets his damage and row. The rest **hide**: into the nearest hut (body gone, a world-space *"3 hiding"* label over the hut — raiders never touch hiders) or, with no huts, crouch 10 m beyond the fire away from the raiders. Armed defenders hold at the gate or breach nearest the raiders (walled) or 8 m out from the fire (unwalled) and fight only inside the perimeter. Rows: *"fetching a spear"*, *"defending, iron spear"*, *"hiding in the hut"*, *"crouching, no spear"*, *"on the tower"*. When the raid ends the hiders come out and held spears go straight back to the store for now (phase 12 walks them back); a save during a raid also returns them. LIFE panel: *Put 3 spears in store*.
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 9: raiders get health and fight back.** Raiders have 3 hp (`Combat/RaidFightTuning`): a stone spear jab does 1, iron 1.5 (≈3 jabs stone, 2 iron). An **armed** hand (a hunter out with his spear; phase 10 adds spears from the store) within the defend perimeter — inside the walls when walled (the raiders' own route test), else 30 m of the fire — drops any load, walks to the nearest raider and jabs with the hunting pose; defenders never chase past the perimeter. A raider who is hit stops stealing and fights his attacker (a swing every 1.2 s); three hits and the villager goes **down** and the drag-to-hut takes over (a death reads *KilledInRaid*). A killed raider drops his loot where he falls (a ground load, collected like any other) and his body fades. When half the landed party is down the rest run for the ship (`Flee`), banner *"the raiders are running"*. Rows read *"defending, stone spear"*. Life events `KilledRaider` / `DefendedCamp` with story lines. LIFE panel: *Start raid now*, *Arm (stone spear)*.
- 2026-09-30 — **Ore outcrops: iron is reachable without an ore island.** Kevin: *"implement the ore into the game, so one can make iron."* Ore was an island KIND only (unlocked from 0.55 of the 3000 m discovery radius, ~1650 m out), so nowhere Kevin had sailed offered ore and the forge's iron recipe (2 ore a bar, fire II) could not run. Now the populator stands a small **ore outcrop** (5 Ore rocks x 4 units = 20 ore = 10 iron; no regrowth) on the nearest island to the start point (always), on every **large** island (mean radius >= 100 m, added 2026-10-03, see the log) and on one in two of the others that get resources (non-Ore, not shelter-only; was a third until 2026-10-03; hash of the island centre, `WantsOreOutcrop`; no `Random` draw, the build's stream is put back, so the world is identical and an old save's islands do not change). `Island.HasOreOutcrop` marks it; `Outpost.EnsureOreStock` books exactly the rocks into the ledger (also for a camp restored from an old save) so the Gather menu, `GatherSync` and the gather party offer Ore; `PlaceCampStone` stands the ore rocks in the ring round the fire, a turn round from the boulders. Ore islands (ring >= 0.55) still hold the real seams. **The first camp's island always has ore too (Kevin: "Give Island_6 ore too", same day):** the first camp the player made (`OutpostLedger.firstCamp`, decided once and saved) gets 3 ore rocks (12 ore) laid at camp time by `Outpost.EnsureHomeOre` on ground `FreeGroundFor` calls free (clear of buildings and keep-outs, blueprints, walls, roads, ladders, the fire's 6 m ring, other rocks; open ground the fire can reach, so inside a closed palisade; above the beach), the spots saved in the ledger (`homeOreX/Z`) so they never shift; old saves get it on the next load. The populator outcrop's ring placement uses the same clearance test. Unplayed.
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 8: overboard modules in the shipyard.** Five new slot-catalog fittings (`Resources/ShipModules/Catalog/module.*.json`, placement *inner*, built free and instant like the others) plus the existing lookout: **bulwarks** (rail grip loss ×0.6 each, up to 2), **safety lines** (grip loss ×0.15 but crew work ×0.85 — guns, sails, oars), **lifebuoy rack** (+6 m throw reach; the thrown buoy adds 45 s to the swimmer once), **scramble net** (haul time ×0.5), **jolly boat** (big ships: 2+ middle sections, dock level 4 — under 3 m/s it launches, rows out on the waves, brings the swimmer or crate back; one hand out while it's away), **lookout** (warning 0.8 s earlier, swimmers last 30 s longer). Read through `Ship/Overboard/OverboardModules.cs`; knobs in `OverboardTuning`. Slot self-test 11 catalog entries, green. Art and icons are stand-ins for Astra.
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 7: recruits at sea and ferrying.** Washed-ashore crew and **strangers** now wait on island beaches as visible people beside a smoking signal fire (`World/Life/CastawayField.cs`), shown within `RecruitTuning.castawayShowMetres` (250 m). A camp-less island rolls a stranger once, deterministically from its name (`strangerChance` 0.25, at most 3 alive), the first time the ship comes near — so old saves gain them. Within 45 m and slow, a big bottom **Take <name> aboard** button (`Ship/Overboard/CastawayHud.cs`) boards them after a 3 s fill if there is a free berth (`Outpost.BerthRefusal`), else *"No free berth"*. **Ferrying** already worked end to end (drag a hand aboard at camp A, drop them ashore at camp B); it now logs a `Ferried` life event and story line when a hand lands at a camp other than their first. Saved: castaways, stranger names, islands already rolled.
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 6: cargo overboard.** A per-ship **lashing** meter (`Ship/Overboard/CargoLashing.cs`) drains in rough water (a higher threshold than crew grip), heel and slams, ×storm; at zero a crate of up to 4 units of a held resource (weighted by count) slides off the low side (`VoyageManager.RemoveLoot`), banner *"Cargo overboard — 4 timber"*. Never before the scripted first man overboard, never in calm water, never offline. The crate floats on the real waves (`FloatingCargo`, drawn with `CargoVisual`) for 120 s, settles, then sinks (*"The timber sank"*). The same pickup brings it back: rings, edge arrows, tap to steer, and one **Throw line to …** button per reachable target (people first), each claiming a different free hand — so a person and a crate in the water together is a real choice. Recovered cargo always goes back aboard, even over capacity. A save while a crate floats puts it back in the hold (nothing vanishes through a save).
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 5: man overboard.** Kevin's favourite (`docs/PLAN-DEATH-RESCUE.md`). **5a:** seasick crew now WALK to the rail to heave (their station empties meanwhile — GDD §3's "guns fall silent one at a time"). Each crew member has a **grip** meter (`CrewAgent.TrackGrip`): drains from `SmoothnessMeter.Roughness01` above a calm threshold, heel, hull slams, ×storm ×night, ÷sea legs (`LifeRecord.seaLegs`); refills in calm, so smooth sailing in calm water is zero risk. At the rail, low grip → *"Hold on!"* + haptic, then the fall: splash, *MAN OVERBOARD!* banner, haptic, 0.5 s slow-down, and a **swimmer** (`Ship/Overboard/Swimmer.cs`) riding the real waves with a light and a timer (`OverboardTuning`: 150 s calm, shorter in storm/night). Timeout near land → **washed ashore** (saved `castaways`, fetched in phase 7); in open water → **lost at sea** (sea grave, placed at the next watched camp). The **first** man overboard is scripted in calm water with a 240 s timer (`firstOverboardDone`). Haptics via a native UIKit feedback-generator plugin (`Assets/Plugins/iOS/SeaSickHaptics.mm`, `OverboardHaptics`) — never `Handheld.Vibrate`. Dead and castaway names never come back on load. **5b:** a countdown ring on the water, an edge arrow with the same ring and distance when off screen, **tap the swimmer (or the arrow) → the heading autopilot steers to them**; the throttle stays Kevin's — a drag within 50° of the swimmer only sets speed, a stop tap keeps the steer (coast in), a drag clearly elsewhere takes the helm back. Within 9 m of the hull under 1.8 m/s a big **Throw line** button appears (else "slow down" / "No free hands"); the nearest free crew member walks to the rail and hauls for 4 s along a drawn line (slips if the ship pulls away); rescued = soaked and shaken (sickness spike, off stations a while, +sea legs, life events on both). All screens are IMGUI stand-ins in `Ship/Overboard/` for Astra.
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 4: pout and the floor.** Kevin (going to bed): *"work through this so i can playtest in the morning."* An angry hand (mood < 0.5) walks to the fire and pouts for `LifeTuning.poutSeconds` (300 real s), then goes back to his order; `poutCooldownSeconds` (600) before the next. Pouts start and tick only while the camp is watched and running. While pouting he does no work but still eats (a fed hand cheers up normally); a load in his arms is delivered if he is at the drop, else dropped as a ground load. **The floor:** `minHandsFloor` (2) — a pout never leaves fewer than that many working hands. Row: *"pouting at the fire · 3:12"*. **Open for Kevin:** the old below-half-mood work slow-down is KEPT alongside the pout (replacing it would shift the food balance); plain idle pose facing the fire (no arms-crossed pose exists). **[2026-10-02: assignments are permanent, Age-of-Empires style — mood and hunger never stop work and nobody pouts; see the 2026-10-02 decisions-log entry "Assignments are permanent".]**
- 2026-09-28 — **Death/rescue phase 3: the tombstone.** Kevin: *"go ahead with phase 3."* A grave not yet placed is pending; while its camp is watched (or any camp, for a death at sea) the forced flow runs, oldest first (`World/Life/GravePlacementFlow.cs`): a ghost tombstone near the fire, **tap the ground to move it** (red where `Outpost.CanPlace` refuses), one big bottom button *"Lay <name> to rest"*, no cancel. `GraveGate.Blocking` gates the ashore tap router (`IslandInput`), new builds (`Outpost.Raise`, player path only) and casting off (`AnchorController.WeighAnchor`); camera pan/zoom stay free and the game keeps running. Placed graves save x, z and yaw and are restored exactly (buildings-never-move) from `Outpost.Adopt`, and reserve their ground against later siting. Four primitive variants (slab, cross, cairn, leaning board) picked by name hash. Tapping a tombstone opens an IMGUI stand-in **story card** (name, days, cause, three sentences, *All graves* / *Close*); *All graves* lists every grave. Screens inside `Scripts/UI` (ledger drawer, build menu) are not gated yet — sent to Astra.
- 2026-09-27 — **Death/rescue phase 2: downed, dragged to a hut, loads dropped where they fall.** Kevin: *"go ahead with phase 2."* A hand who goes down or dies drops any picked-up load on the ground where he is (`OutpostLedger.groundLoads`, saved; drawn with the camp pile props by `GroundLoadPiles`); an idle hand's first hauling chore is carrying ground loads to the store, as an ordinary walked trip (`HaulPlace.Ground`). While a camp is watched, the nearest free hand (idle first; a carrier drops his load) is sent to a downed hand (`DispatchRescuers`); once he reaches him the death timer stops for good, and he drags him (`LifeTuning.dragSpeed` 1.2 m/s, `CampWorker.Rescue.cs`) to the nearest hut door, or the fire, where he recovers for `recoverDays` (≈2 real minutes, ticks in game time so it also finishes while away). The drag freezes when the camp is not watched. Hunting accidents: `huntAccidentChance` 3 % per kill (iron spear half), rolled only while watched. Rows read "down · 2:31", "running to Anna", "dragging Anna", "recovering in the hut" via `OutpostHand.Doing`. The dev LIFE panel gained **Accident** and per-hand state. The drag is a straight line, not routed (it can cross a wall); refine if it shows.
- 2026-09-27 — **Death, rescue and repopulation; phase 1 built (life log + death pipeline).** Kevin's brainstorm decisions (`docs/PLAN-DEATH-RESCUE.md`): deaths only from things you can see coming and answer, *always downed before dead*, a forced tombstone with a three-sentence life story, pout instead of desertion, a floor, and repopulation by huts, sea recruits and ferrying — *"yes to all, go ahead with phase 1."* Built: `World/Life/` (`LifeTuning`, `Lives` registry + `LifeEvents`, `LifeRecord`/`GraveRecord`, `LifeStory` with FNV-picked templates), `OutpostHand.downed/downedLeft/downedCause`, `OutpostLedger.Down/Revive/Die/TickDowned` (watched + unpaused + not catching up only), save lists `lives` and `graveyard` (no version bump; old saves read empty), dead names skipped by `VillagerNames.NextFor`, a flat downed pose in `CampWorker`, and a dev-build-only **LIFE** panel (top-left) with Down / Kill / Revive per hand and the last story. A carried load still goes to the store on death until phase 2 drops it where they fell. Stories name the island (`Island_2`, "the home island").
- 2026-09-27 — **Delivery on arrival: carrying is physical, no trip timers.** Kevin: *"I don't want walking time to be factored into building something, in fact, I don't want time to play a role in any construction or whatever ... Only when a villager has delivered the object will the object be counted… obviously if they're standing still and working at a station a timer should exist such as '56 seconds until spear'."* Hunting: *"walking up to an animal, jabbing it with its spear, picking it up and walking back."* Unwatched camps: **invisible walkers** (his option 1) — the same villagers walk the same routes at walking speed without being drawn; no catch-up formula. Stock changes only at pickup and drop-off; stationary work keeps its timers; walking is not slowed by hunger. The siting walk label is gone. Plan and invariants: `docs/DELIVERY-ON-ARRIVAL.md`, `docs/DEV-TOOLS.md` (2026-09-27 section).
- 2026-09-27 — **Economy first pass: real prices, one tuning file, hammer-only site timer, hide only from hunting.** Kevin: *"1. sure, go ahead and put in some real numbers. 2. …we are just talking about building time here, so that timer is only active/relevant when resources are in place and someone is working on the building. 4. ok add them there (one tuning file + the main ones in FEEL). …it is fine that hide is the only way to reach campfire 2, as long as the hide can't be gathered."* Playtest cap removed; prices, hammer seconds and the pacing reasoning in §6 "Economy numbers"; `EconomyTuning.asset` + FEEL `EconomyFeel`; the site says who is doing what (*"x is gathering resources for the build, or y is building"*). **First pass, tune by play; unplayed.**
- 2026-09-27 — **The gather party: fetch raw goods from an island without making camp.** Kevin: *"yes go ahead with the gather party."* Anchored off an island with **no camp**, the anchor prompt offers **⛏ Send gather party** beside 🔥 Make camp. A bottom sheet (`UI/Sheets/GatherPartySheet.cs`, `Resources/UI/GatherParty.uss`, big thumb chips) asks **what** (the raw goods with a source in reach of the landing — Stone incl. loose scenery rocks, Timber, Ore, Spice, Food — with the units in reach), **how much** (5 / 10 / 20 / Fill hold) and **how many hands** (1..hands aboard; at anchor she needs no minimum sailing crew), then Send. The hands walk down the plank; each goes to the nearest free source **reachable from the landing** (within 80 m, a straight line that `Walkability.MayStep` passes every 1.5 m and that crosses no water — there is no camp path grid without a camp), works it at Kevin's dials (**wood 5 s a log, stone 8 s, ore 10 s, per unit**), carries the whole armful (a tree is 1 log, a rock its 2/4/8, at most 8 and never more than the hold takes) back over the plank **into the ship's hold**. Trip time is the walk itself. They stop when the amount is in, the hold is full, nothing reachable is left, raiders come near, or the player taps **Recall** (the prompt reads *"Stone 7/20 · 3 ashore"*); recalled hands walk back and a load in hand goes in the hold when they board. **Rules chosen:** *sailing* — the ship is `Ashore` while any hand is out and `Ashore` offers no cast-off, the prompt says *"N hands ashore — recall first to cast off"* with Recall in its place; *save* — a party is not saved: the save writes the ship's crew by name and the hold, so a party ashore loads as **recalled** (every hand aboard, at most one armful each in hand dropped), and no hand can be lost; *raids* — raiders within 30 m of any hand, or a raid landing on this island, and the party runs for the ship (a recall); a party is refused while an enemy ship is at the island; *unwatched* — a party only exists while she lies there, which is always watched, so there is no offline stepping; *food* — there is no ship ration logic yet, so a party eats nothing extra. **Nothing stays:** no fire, pile, upkeep or outpost; the island's survey `Outpost` is untouched (`HasCamp` stays false) and its ledger is not written. **What persists (2026-09-27, fixed same day):** every source a hand cuts is booked **by name** into the island's own ledger (the survey `Outpost`'s on a camp-less island — the same ledger a camp made there later keeps): a loose rock by its index in the island's deterministic rock index, a tree by its scenery index, a kit deposit or ore/spice prop by the decimetre XZ it was made at (`OutpostLedger.GroundTaken.cs`: `takenRocks`, `takenTrees`, `takenPropX/Z`, saved with the ledger, empty in an old save). Its **whole yield** leaves the island's stock — `standing` **and** `standingMax` together — so the camp's count-derived picture (`GatherSync`/`SyncFelling`, `taken = standingMax − standing`, nearest the camp first) is unchanged and hides nothing extra, and every camp order (`GatherSync`, the fell order, `SceneryStone`'s nodes, the seam sizing) leaves the named sources out. `GroundTaken.Apply` (in `CatchUp`) draws the named set gone — rock hidden, tree felled with the plain `Fell` (never stood back up), a node on it marked taken. **One rule:** named takes are hidden by name, camp takes by count over what is left, so neither hides what the other took nor shows it. `SaveGame` keeps a camp-less ledger that has named takes; a later camp starts from the reduced stock. Taken spice does not regrow (its ceiling went with it). A party is refused while the island is still being surveyed (no books to write in yet). Check: `GatherPartyCheck.Run("Island_2")` in play mode, then `GatherPartyCheck.Report`; persistence gates (books, picture, island unload/reload, save → `LoadSlotAndReload`): `GatherPartyCheck.Run("<camp-less island>", true)`. **Dials are guesses; unplayed.**
- 2026-09-27 — **Camp › Overview: the goal chain (concept C).** Kevin, on the Melvor-style mockups: *"this looks good, the UI that is."* A tap on the campfire now opens the camp **overview** (`CampOverviewSheet`, a tall sheet) instead of the old camp sheet's first page. **NEXT GOAL**: the fire's next level, one line of why, a bar and *"2 of 3 ready"*. Then the **chain** of what it waits on: each price line have/need; under a short one, how it is got (a recipe at a station, the ground here, the hunt, or *"not on this island · ore is on the far islands"*) and that station's state (built? a worker? a tool? an order?) — at most 4 deep, a met line one green row, a red border on what blocks. Then **First step** with a **Go** button that opens the sheet that fixes it (the station, an idle hand, the build list, or Raise when all is met). Below it — or on a second segment (Goal / Today) when the band is short, never a pager — the campfire card (have/need tiles + Raise), BEING BUILT and TODAY (food days, stone left on the island, the watch). The engine is pure and reusable for a building's upgrade or any price (a ship section later): `World/Economy/GoalChain.cs`, checked by `Dev/Editor/GoalChainCheck.cs` against Kevin's Island_2 (hide 0/4 → hunting → spear → forge with no smith). The old camp sheet stays one tap away (the overview's ☰ fallback, and every Go that needs the build list or the hands) until the drawer's pages replace it. Styles: `Resources/UI/Overview.uss`.
- 2026-09-27 — **Land HUD after Melvor Idle: the ledger drawer and the alert strip.** Kevin, on the Melvor-style mockups: *"this looks good, the UI that is."* Ashore, the four bottom buttons (Build / Crew / Stores / Ship) are gone; a **☰ button** at the top-left, beside the resource bar, slides in the **ledger drawer** from the left (~81 % of the phone's width, capped on a desk; dim scrim over the world; closes on ✕, a tap on the scrim, a swipe left, or by opening anything). Header: the island's name, *"Day N · campfire L · N hands"*, a gear (the Pause/Settings menu) and ✕. Four framed groups, each row 44 units tall with an icon, a name, a coloured status and (where there is progress) a thin bar: **CAMP** — Overview (the camp overview sheet: goal chain + campfire; counts the alerts), Stores (the bank), People (the hands), Build (the build list); **GATHER** — one row per seam the island has (Timber, Stone, Food, Hunting, Ore/Spice where present): who is on it, or why it is stuck (`OutpostLedger.StallReason`), red when stalled / worked out / no spear, bar = how full that pile is; **MAKE** — one row per production building INSTANCE (Sawmill, Forge, Kitchen, Fletcher, Quarry, Farm): the worker or *"no smith"*, green working / amber no worker or no order / red stuck, bar = the job on the bench; a plan the fire has not unlocked yet shows grey with *"campfire II"*, an unlocked one not yet built grey with *"not built"*; **SEA** — Ship (the manifest), Chart. A row opens its sheet (a MAKE row its station page). Under the resource bar, the **alert strip**: up to three tappable chips, most urgent first — the raid (*"RAID · 3 ashore"*, ember), no food / under a day's food, nobody on watch, a stuck hand (*"Tam · no stone left"*), *"No spear · no hunting"*, idle hands, builders short, angry hands — each opening where it is fixed (the lookout, the hand, the forge, the build list). The raid is said by the strip ashore, so the old raid banner stays quiet there (it keeps the after-raid verdict, and it is still the voice at sea). Tapping things in the world still opens their sheets; at sea the sea HUD is unchanged — the drawer is ashore-only for now. Code: `UI/Sheets/LedgerDrawer.cs`, `AlertStrip.cs`, `CampAlerts.cs`, `Resources/UI/Ledger.uss`.
- 2026-09-27 — **A production building opens one full-height station page (concept A2).** Kevin, on the Melvor-style mockups: *"this looks good, the UI that is."* A tap on a sawmill, forge, kitchen, fletcher or quarry (or its MAKE row in the ledger drawer) opens a tall page instead of the paged band: **header** — ☰ (the ledger drawer), the name, *"Level N · island"*, a status pill (working / waiting / no worker / output full); the **worker card** (initial, name, *"sawyer · content"*, Assign / Swap / Unassign); **RECIPES** — a two-column grid of cards (the item's icon on a dark tile, name ×yield, inputs with icons and the bench time at THIS building's level, and on the running one a bar and *"making 7 of 10"*); a locked card is dashed and says why (*"needs campfire II and a saw blade"*), and a level-2 recipe is gated by this building's OWN level; more cards than the band holds page with dots (the forge's five: two a page on the phone), never a scroll; a tap on a card makes it the order's recipe (a running order switches at once, keeping its amount); **ORDER · BOARDS** — one segmented control ∞ / 5 / 10 / 20 / Stop; **IN AND OUT** — bay n/cap *waiting* → bench ring % *on the bench* → rack n/cap *finished*, all of this instance's `StationStock`; the stall reason when stopped; and the **upgrade card** at the bottom (*"Level 2 · 1.5× faster"*, have/need per item, one button that says Upgrade or what is missing — *"Campfire II"*) that raises and retints only this building. The farm is the same page with BEDS (ripe pips, next bed) and FOOD OUT in place of recipes and the order. Sized to fit the phone's tall band without scrolling (Kevin's no-scroll rule stands); the shorter desk column scrolls with the wheel. Code: `UI/Sheets/StationSheet.cs` (+ the shared `StationPage` kit), `FarmSheet.cs`, `Resources/UI/Station.uss`; the old dropdown layout (`StationProduction.cs`) is gone.
- 2026-09-27 — **Goals beyond the campfire: "Set as goal".** Kevin: *"let's try it."* The station page's upgrade card carries a **Set as goal** pill while the upgrade cannot be paid yet (a second tap clears it); the goal is THAT building's next level (per instance, by plan + ordinal). - 2026-09-27 — **Camp › Build and Camp › People are their own tall pages.** Kevin approved the Ledger follow-ups (the Melvor redesign he called *"this looks good, the UI that is"*). The drawer's CAMP rows **Build** and **People** no longer open the old camp sheet's tabs. **Build** (`BuildSheet`): a segmented control **Shelter · Food · Industry · Defence · Sea**, then one card per plan in that group — a building glyph, the name, its one-line blurb, the price of the NEXT copy as have/need item icons (red when short), and the copies (*"×2 of 3 — 4th at campfire III"*, `Techs.Caps` + `BuildPlans.PriceForCopy`). Order: can build (ice outline), short (still tappable: the builders fetch and cut what is missing, as before; carries **Set as goal**, `GoalPin.SetBuild`), at its cap (dimmed); plans the fire has not opened are grouped under *NEEDS CAMPFIRE II*, dashed, not tappable. A tap on a card closes the page and starts siting (`CampSiting.Begin`, the same call the old build rows made). Defence also carries the **palisade** (the wall tool) and the **ladder** (foot, then top), and says how a gate is put in. Opens on the tab it was last left on; the no-spear alert opens it on Industry. **People** (`PeopleSheet`): header *"5 / 8 beds · 3 warm"*; chips **All / Stuck / Unhappy** with counts (stuck = the alert strip's rule: idle, stalled, body-blocked, or a hunter with no spear; unhappy = hungry or angry); a row per hand — initial, name, the job with its place (*"sawyer at the sawmill"*, *"gathering stone"*, *"builder · shelter"*), mood + bed (*"content · warm"*, *"hungry · cold"*, *"angry · no bed"*), the stall reason in red; a tap opens the hand's own sheet. Every route that opened the old build list or hands tab now lands here (drawer rows, alert chips, the MAKE group's "not built" rows, the overview's Go). A group of at most five cards fits the phone's tall band; the list scrolls (clamped) only as the fallback on a short screen. Code: `UI/Sheets/BuildSheet.cs`, `PeopleSheet.cs`, `CampPages.cs`, `Resources/UI/CampPages.uss`. **Unplayed.**
- 2026-09-27 — **The rocks you see on an island are stone.** Kevin: *"there are so many rocks on the island but I guess they don't qualify as a stone resource, please change so they do."* The island bake now indexes every **loose** rock it stamps (`SceneryRocks`): lone inland boulders, the scree at the foot of an outcrop, tideline boulders on dry beach, and Astra's `Boulder_*` accents (the stones beside trees and on outcrop edges, Island_2). **Landform stays scenery:** cliff shards and every outcrop/headland/sea stack built from them, her `Cliff_*` slab and talus, washed beach stones, boulders in the surf, anything over 6 m across. A camp stands a Stone node on each loose rock inside its path grid, above the beach and **reachable from the fire** (`CampPath.Reachable`; at most 240, nearest first), so the ledger, gather trips, plot clearing and the Stores see them with no new path. **Yield by size — PROVISIONAL:** under 1.5 m across **2 stone**, under 2.8 m **4**, larger **8**. Worked out, a rock **vanishes** (no remnant); which rocks are gone is the camp's Stone seam drawn nearest-the-camp-first (`GatherSync`), so it is saved with the books and stays gone across save/load and island reload — nothing new is saved. The seam is re-sized once (sizing v2) to kit deposits + these rocks, only ever raised: **stone is now plentiful wherever rocks are**, and the stone-exhausted Island_2 (0 standing) gets its visible rocks back as stone, all standing. Rocks stay obstacles (D4): they block the path grid, and a rock on a build plot is cleared by the builders (2 stone a rock, same as a kit deposit). Hiding a rock collapses its vertices in the welded cell mesh; one mesh upload per dirty cell per frame. Home's authored dressing indexes no rocks (unchanged). Check: `SceneryStoneCheck.Run("Island_2")`, then `Start`/`Report`/`Reload`, play mode.
- 2026-09-27 — **The island camera's angle locks; only distance, spin and pan stay in your hands.** Kevin: *"I would like to try to lock in the camera at a certain angle that looks good when on the island. so I can still go up and down and spin around but the actual angle is what I would like to be a good set one."* Locked mode (`IslandCamLock`, default ON) renders every island shot — hands-on or the authored dock/ring framing alike — at one fixed tilt, 38° by default, at every zoom; the two-finger/right-drag/PageUp-PageDown tilt gesture goes inert, and a hill in the way is cleared by standing the seat further back along the same ray instead of tipping the lens at it. The old curve-plus-hand-tilt behaviour is still there, off a toggle. `angleDeg` (20°–70°) and the `locked` toggle are on the phone in FeelLab (the FEEL panel), so Kevin can find the number he likes and send it back to bake as the default.
- 2026-09-27 — **Combat on the phone: a lock button, and the guns fire themselves while locked.** Kevin (iPhone): *"I can't press the button at sea that lets me lock on to the enemy. also, cannons should auto fire when engaged."* The lock prompt was a keyboard label (the phone's tap-the-hull path left with tap-to-sail in f858d54), so the lock and its two-ship camera were desk-only. First fix: a ≥44 pt **Lock on / Release** button in the shared bottom-centre prompt slot, plus a tap on an enemy in the top half; upright the lock camera may swing 140° (78° on a desk) so both hulls stay in the tall frame. Kevin, same day, on that button: *"the click to lock button should appear somewhere better on the screen where it's easier to press. even pressing the ship should work."* It now has its own fixed spot — a large (~72 pt) round button at the bottom-right, above the safe area, clear of the wheel, the broadside buttons and the point-of-sail panel — rather than sharing the contested prompt slot with "come alongside"; it shows a reticle + range while a target is in view, pulses once when a new one comes into range, and turns ember/red with "Release" once locked. A tap locks the enemy anywhere on screen now, not just the top half, with a generous (~60 pt) hit radius around the hull — a tap on water still does nothing, and a tap on the ship already locked does nothing (release is the button's job). **Locked = engaged:** each gun fires on its own when loaded, manned, laid on the target and in reach, never through a friendly tower; steering to bring a side to bear is the whole of the gunnery. Q/E and the broadside buttons stay as fire-now overrides.
- 2026-09-29 — **Sailing presentation:** the sailing camera must preserve a visible horizon for navigation and reading the sea. Camera angle comparisons retain that constraint. Compact tap-to-open chart, readable portrait controls, restrained foam/wake, and warm ship lighting remain the visual direction. Water appearance changes must preserve the wave simulation and have a restorable source backup.
- 2026-09-29 — **Approved F coaster replaces the sailing prototype ship.** Fixed beam for now; independently one or two decks on stern, repeatable middles and bow. Widening and further storeys wait for design. Taller stern grows paddle diameter without requiring a wider hull. Helm stays clear; raised stern guns are below it, and middle/bow have lower and upper cannon positions. Compact ladders and exposed-end walls follow neighbouring deck heights. Storage and berths remain abstract allocations. The 3D section builder retains one atomic confirmation; new cannons can be built free for the current prototype. Warm lantern lighting and the carved wood/deep navy art are integrated. Legacy voyages retain their section count and equipment through migration. See `docs/implementation/F-COASTER.md` for implementation and measured verification.
- 2026-09-27 — **Shipyard: Astra's upper decks imported before her review — third deck and gun foredeck.** Kevin: *"import whatever assets Astra has made (even if she hasn't pushed them; if they need fixing later I'll deal with that), so that includes … the upper decks"*. The **third deck** (art-staging/modular-third-layer-v1) fits per section on a *connected* raised stern/middle/bow (not the end-wall variants): +1.22 m walking deck at authoring Z 6.64, adds hold and berths to that section, weighs 2.7–5.0 t per section and raises her CG (full three-section third deck on the raised long ship: GM 2.45 → 1.98 m in the planner). The **gun foredeck** (modular-foredeck-v1, narrow) fits the standard V3 bow: an open platform with two extra gun slots, ~1.1 t, no hold/berths, no freeboard change. Both are a "None / Third deck" or "None / Foredeck" row on the section sheet's Structure page, and every draft carrying one shows an ART_UNREVIEWED warning (crew headroom, access/edge guards on a partial third deck, gun recoil, colliders and iPhone cost are not certified). Detail: `docs/MODULAR-SHIPS.md` §14.
- 2026-09-26 — **Shipyard: "Raise all / Lower all" removed from the overview; raising stays per section.** Kevin: *"each section costs a substantial amount of resources, so raising 4 at the same time is unrealistic for the pace of the game."* The whole-hull shortcut let a player commit every section's raise cost in one tap, which reads as free next to the real per-section cost of a raise; the Low/Raised toggle inside each section's own sheet (`ShipyardSectionSheet`) is the only way to raise a deck now. `ShipyardDraft.RaiseAll`/`LowerAll`/`SetRaisedDeck` stay in code — `ShipyardUiProbe` still exercises them directly — they are just no longer reachable from the UI.
- 2026-09-25 — **Modular ships & shipyard: six decisions from the refit/section-editing pass.** Detail: `docs/SHIPYARD-API.md` §15–18, `docs/RAISED-DECK.md`, `docs/RAISED-SECTIONS.md`, `docs/SHIPYARD-SECTIONS-UI.md`.
  - **Surplus hands go ashore, never lost.** A refit that drops berths below hands aboard is never refused for crew: the extra hands land as real, named villagers at the home berth's island (report warns "N hand(s) will go ashore"). The refit itself is refused only if she isn't at her home berth (`HANDS_ASHORE_NEED_HOME`).
  - **Refits happen only at the HOME BERTH, and the berth can move.** "Make this my home berth" moves the ship's home dock (and dry dock) to any player-built pier. Kevin: *"make my home berth the pier I built at island_2 since that's a better island for me to do my building."* The home settlement itself doesn't move — only where she refits.
  - **Guns are deck equipment, not hull.** Cannons are `equipment.deck-gun` modules fit into fixed port/starboard deck slots, not hull-form sockets. A removed gun goes to the dry dock, never destroyed, and a later refit can fit it back. No hull gun ports — deck guns only.
  - **Beam: standard or wide (W1x, 12.08 u).** Ship-wide toggle; a raised deck needs the wide beam, and the toggle refuses narrowing while any section is raised.
  - **Raised decks are chosen per SECTION.** Kevin: *"I should be able to select different sections so I can decide which sections to raise."* Each section (stern/middle/bow) is independently LOW or RAISED. The between-deck this makes (1.22 m clear) is enclosed and never walkable — crew, passages and guns for a raised section live on the upper deck, and a raised section next to a low one closes with an end wall + stairs. Raising a section adds hold, berths and mass, and raises her CG — but sea trials show a raised ship actually rolls LESS (heavier, deeper draft outweighs the higher CG). Raised ends over a low middle still cut cargo allowance by ~2 t, because the low middle's deck line caps the waterline for the whole hull.
  - **Shipyard UI: overview → section → sheet → one Confirm.** Tap a section tile to open its sheet (Structure / Guns / Interior swipe pages); [Done] returns to the overview; "+" tiles insert a section, removing one is done by selecting it. One [Confirm refit] applies everything atomically. Interior splits each section's own space budget between berths and hold (a berth = half a hold cell); defaults reproduce today's numbers. Voyage stores (food/fuel aboard) and refit costs/build time are deferred.
- 2026-09-23 — **Station stock: storage is the hub, nothing is worked without an order.** Follows directly from the production-chains entry below. Before a Storage/Storehouse is built, gathered goods sit in a square beside the campfire; once built, they move inside it (visuals may cap at extreme counts; the true count never does). All gathered resources go to storage, never straight to a station — when storage is full, gatherers stop and find other tasks. Each production station (sawmill, quarry, fletcher, kitchen, blacksmith) now owns real stock: an input bay, a one-job bench and an output rack, sized to the station (sawmill 6 logs/12 planks, quarry 5 stone/12 brick). **Nothing is worked without a player order** — make N (5/10/20/50/100) or repeat until stopped. Hauling is by the armful: a raw bay that runs dry is refilled by an idle villager or the station's own worker from storage; if storage has none, the stationed worker gathers it himself when it's gatherable, else he waits ("waiting for X (made at Y)"); finished goods move rack → storage when the rack is full, when a hauler is idle, or when the worker runs out of raw. Builders draw storage first, station racks second. Station stock counts in camp totals and every building always shows its true stock. Shipped in "Station stock: bays, benches, racks, player orders, armful hauling" (`OutpostLedger.Stations.cs`, `StationStock.cs`).
- 2026-09-23 — **Production chains, three tiers, fire levels I+II shipped, hard gate on hunting spears.** Kevin: *"there should be steps to things ... to hunt, you need a spear so the blacksmith needs a wood plank for the shaft and stone or metal for the tip ... the classic level up campfire to level up buildings ... this makes sure that you need to get a wide variety of resources to progress."* Raw (gathered/hunted) → treated (made at a station) → items (made, used, worn out); nothing takes an item as input. Fire level I (camp): free, base, gathering+building+cooking. Fire level II (hamlet): 10 boards + 6 stone + 4 hide, raises fire at once, opens quarry and all building level 2s, unlocks iron+fine boards recipes. Hide is the first treated-goods gate and comes off hunting (one per animal killed). Hunting needs a spear (stone or iron); stone spear wears 0.25 per kill (4 kills), iron spear 1/12 per kill (12 kills). The spear is a hard gate: no spear in hand, no hunting, no hide, no path forward without the blacksmith. `RecipeGraph.Validate()` refuses any build where anything is unreachable from a fresh camp; it logs on load in dev/editor builds. `docs/PRODUCTION-CHAINS.md` is generated from the data. Player sees the ladder through station sheets: each sheet lists recipes (locked/unlocked) and names what blocks each step; recipes unlock by fire level and station level. Building level 2 costs are in Techs.Upgrades (fire level II gate, 2-6 brick + 2-4 fine boards per building). **Decision:** hard gate and hide now, coal/tools/spice later at fire III/IV (shipped first, designed later); crafting happens by station sheet (StationSheet), not a central workshop; fire I+II shipped, III+IV reserved.
- 2026-09-23 (later the same phone session) — **Build sites stop over-delivering, gathering becomes a walk, and it's all one ledger.** Three Kevin notes from the same playtest, all "we finetune later": *"builders carried far too much, the x/y counts did not match what arrived, and the percentage rose before the quota"*; and, from the earlier sway/haul pass, *"storage trips should be based on the time it takes to walk to the storage (or wood source) and back, not some arbitrary timer. cutting logs should take 5 seconds."* **Sites:** stocking is whole-armful trips capped at what the site still needs net of loads already in transit (`HaulPlace.Site`) — never two haulers bringing the last 3, never a shown count that isn't whole units in hand. `Progress01`/hammering stay at 0% until every material reads full (5/5, 3/3, ...) *and* the plot's trees/rocks are cleared; an old save's over-delivered row gives its surplus back to the store on load rather than keeping phantom stock. **Trip timing:** every haul — station, site, or a stationed worker fetching his own raw — is now ledger arithmetic over real positions: `2 × leg × PathFactor (1.15) / WalkMetresPerSecond (2.6 m/s) + HandleSeconds (3 s)`, plus cutting time at the pickup if it's the island. `StationTripDays`/`SiteTripDays`/`HaulPerHandPerDay` (flat per-day rates) are gone; the route is saved on the hauling hand so the villager you see mimes the exact leg the books paid for. Playtest constants, Kevin's to tune, not final: wood 5 s/log, stone 8 s, ore 10 s; clearing a tree off a plot 5 s, a rock 8 s. **Gathering is trips too:** a plain Gather order now walks store → source → store like any other haul, depositing into the store rather than accruing a flat daily rate; a full store sends the gatherer to help haul for the stations or help build until there's room, then he resumes on his own. **Carried loads are visible** — three logs in a villager's arms render as three logs, capped at six for the eye. The point of routing everything through the same trip arithmetic: a watched camp and an unwatched one land on the same books (D2 holds at any tick size), because the villagers only mime what the ledger already decided. Shipped across "Station stock: bays, benches, racks, player orders, armful hauling" through "Trips take real walking time; a log cuts in 5 s; carried loads are visible" (`OutpostLedger.cs`, `OutpostLedger.Stations.cs`, `StationStockSelfTest.cs`); unplayed past Kevin's own phone session.
- 2026-09-22 — **The phone gets its own chase framing, and speed is a dolly rather than a lens.** The desk shot (B2 off the SailShots sheet — 26 m astern, 8 m up, 58 mm) is a landscape composition: it spends the frame's *width* on sea either side of the hull, and held upright that width is gone. `ChaseCamera` now carries a second preset it crossfades to over half a second whenever the window is taller than wide — 24 m astern, 12 m up, 6 m of look-ahead, 68° vertical — which is about 21° of pitch and puts the horizon a fifth of the way down from the top with the hull in the lower third and the sea she is crossing filling the tall middle. There is **no mode**: the camera asks `HudLayout.Wide`, the same question the HUD asks of the window, so the two can never disagree about what portrait means, and rotating the device is a move and not a cut. Landscape is untouched, by construction — every portrait term is multiplied by the blend factor. Upright, **speed reads as a dolly, not as a lens**: distance and height scale together by 1 + 0.30·(order / burn notch), keyed to the *telegraph order* so the seat leads her out instead of trailing the stokers, and because both scale the tilt barely moves and the horizon stays put. The FOV speed boost and surf punch fade to zero in the portrait blend — a moving projection on a screen held a foot from your face is the sickest thing a chase camera can do, and the dolly already says everything they were saying. Cruise pull-in is a landscape move and is likewise blended out. Lying stopped or anchored the seat eases in to 0.85 over about three seconds. On top of all of it, a two-finger pinch while sailing (or the wheel on a desk) scales the seat between 0.6 and 1.6 and **decays back to the authored framing over twenty seconds**, so a look at something on the horizon is a look and not a new camera the player has to undo; it stands down while `IslandCam.Engaged`, which has its own pinch, and ignores touches that began on a HUD control. Every safety is unchanged: water floor, terrain clearance, storm drop and pull-in, lock framing, and the island overview path.
- 2026-09-22 (later session) — **The quarry: rough stone into bricks, saved for a building upgrade that does not exist yet.** Same shape as the sawmill and the forge — `BuildPlans.Quarry`, a `quarryman` position, one Work order, `takes = Res.Stone` / `makes = Res.Brick` at 2 a day — 22 timber and 6 stone to raise, no kit model to wear yet (a three-walled shed with a cut face and a brick stack, extruded like the watchtower and the pier). Bricks are stored and loadable like any other made good — the camp sheet's stores row and the manifest's stop-at list both pick it up the same way they picked up boards and tools. `BuildPlan.brickCost` rides on every plan now (0 everywhere today) so an upgrade can be priced in bricks later without threading a new material through the blueprint, the site sheet and the save all at once — **reserved, nothing spends it yet.**
- 2026-09-22 (later session) — **A camp holds a build QUEUE, not one blueprint.** Kevin, on the phone: *"I want to be able to place more blueprints at once."* `OutpostLedger.pending` became `OutpostLedger.sites`, an ordered list served **oldest first**: each row keeps its own costs, its own `timber/stone/brick` counters and fractional carries, its own position, yaw and plan id. One rule decides everything downstream — `Focus` is the oldest site that is not yet stocked, and it is what `BuilderWants` names a material out of, what `CampWorker` walks a load to, and what the starvation lines read; the moment a site has all its materials in, the haulers move to the next one and the finished row waits for `Outpost.FinishReady` to stand it up (as many as are ready, oldest first). Hand-days spill down the queue within a single tick, so one call covering ten days still equals ten calls covering one. The refusals that went: *"something is already being built here"* (siting) and *"Something is already going up"* (the camp sheet's build page and the old IMGUI list) — the one-of-each rule survives and now covers the queue as well as what is built. The camp page prints one `SheetKit.ListRow` per queued site ("shelter · 2/2 stone, 3/6 logs · Anna on it"; the ones behind the focus say *waiting its turn*), up to four, then *"and N more queued"*; the sheet's band arithmetic counts those rows. A tapped blueprint opens a `SiteSheet` for **that row** — `BuildSite` carries its `PendingBuild` and Add-a-hand / Cancel / Move act on it alone. Two drawings cannot overlap: `Outpost.Clear` treats every queued site as occupied ground, with a single `IgnoreSite` escape for the row being moved or measured. **Save:** no version bump — the old single `pending` key stays in the JSON as a migration slot and `OutpostLedger.MigratePending` (called from `Outpost.Adopt`) lifts it into a one-element queue, so Kevin's existing saves load with their half-built hut intact.
- 2026-09-23 — **Two build phases, a raise that happens in the tick, placement that stops refusing clear ground, and no over-delivery.** Kevin's second phone session, four notes, all four here.
  - **STOCKING then BUILDING.** Kevin: *"first the villagers should gather all the resources necessary to build the building, THEN they start actually building it which shouldn't take super long."* A `PendingBuild` now has `Stocked` (every material in — what `Complete` used to mean) and `built`, hand-days of labour that accrue **only** once it is stocked (`OutpostLedger.PayBuild`). `Complete` = stocked AND raised, and that is what `Outpost.FinishReady` waits for. The labour is `BuildDaysPerHand` (0.5 day) scaled by the plan's total materials over a reference of 6 and clamped to ×0.5..×3 — a 6-log/2-stone hut is 0.67 hand-days, so two builders raise it in a third of a game-day and three in a fifth. Hand-days, so N builders finish in 1/N of the time with no special case. The site says which phase it is in in one string, `PendingBuild.PhaseLine` — *"stocking 3/6 logs, 2/2 stone"* then *"building 40%"* — printed by both the camp page and the drawing's own sheet; `Progress01` is the stocking in its first half and the raising in its second, and the ghost keeps solidifying (0.55 → 0.85 alpha) through the build phase so a drawing going opaque with nobody carrying to it reads as a building going up. **Save:** no version bump. `PendingBuild.phased` is false in every old save; `MigratePending` gives an already-stocked old row its labour outright (it was "done" in that save's own terms and would have raised on the next visit) and starts an unstocked one at zero.
  - **The raise happens in the simulation, not on a tap.** Kevin: *"when the hut was completed it remained a blueprint even after it was completed, until I pressed on it."* `Outpost.CatchUp` is what runs the tick and stands finished sites up, and nothing called it on its own — it rode on a sheet refresh, a Hand order, a crew delivery or an anchor drop. `Outpost.Update` now calls it four times a second while the camp is watched (the tick itself still only advances on its 0.1-day quantum, so this adds reconciliation passes, not simulation).
  - **Placement stopped refusing ground that looks clear.** Three rules were over-strict and one was a ghost. (a) The slope limit was `SettlementSite.BuildableSlope`, 0.176 — a hair under **ten degrees**, the threshold the world survey uses to find a patch big enough for a whole village. On sleek low hills almost nothing is under ten degrees. It is now `Outpost.BuildSlopeDegrees`, **30°**, pulled in from the 38° villagers can actually walk (`CampPath.MaxSlopeDegrees`) because a floor is not a footpath. A cliff is still refused. (b) Building spacing was a flat **6 m** added to each footprint's circumscribed circle on both sides — two 8×5 huts needed 12.4 m between centres. Now 1.5 m, a path between them; the footprint circle already carries the size. (c) The island root carried an **8 m keep-out for the beacon**, which stopped spawning the day before — an invisible disc through the middle of the best ground in the camp. Gone. The pier head's 14 m keep-out stays, because that one is a structure you can see. (d) Every refusal now **names its rule with its number**: *"too steep — 41° across it, and 30° is the limit"*, *"a corner of it is down on the beach"*, *"that is inside the harbour's keep-out (14 m)"*, *"too close to the shelter going up there"*. The corner test reports which corner tripped instead of guessing from the centre's height. Trees never blocked placement and still do not — they come down when the site is raised (`Outpost.RaiseRow`).
  - **No over-delivery.** Kevin: *"if the building needed 1 more wood and all 4 villagers were carrying wood to the building site they deposited the wood even though the amount was already reached."* A hauler's destination is re-asked every step of the walk home: `OutpostLedger.SiteWanting(resource)` is the oldest site still **short of that material**, and null means the camp pile. So three of the four turn mid-walk. `OutpostLedger.BuilderWants` and the starvation lines read `StockingFocus` (oldest site not yet stocked) rather than `Focus` (oldest site not yet finished), which are different questions now that a site can be the focus and want nothing. A builder whose site is stocked walks to the drawing and swings a hammer at it instead of fetching. And nothing can sit above its need: `ReconcileSites` runs at the top of every tick and moves any surplus a body pushed in from outside the tick onto the camp pile — past the fire's ceiling if it has to, because the log exists and nothing may vanish.

- 2026-09-22 (later session) — **No home island, trees regrow away from camp, and the world stops pretending anywhere is safe by default.** Kevin's first playtest of the island loop; every number below is what shipped, not a plan.
  - **No home island.** Kevin: *"I don't want to retain the notion of a home island. I want the player to explore and to make any camps on any island(s) they wish."* Phase 1 shipped: the starting island lost its special rules — herds present, a real resource kind, a hill, and no free 30-unit beach ceiling; open ground keeps **zero** everywhere until a campfire stands. The camp sheet opens there and surveys/wakes/stows hands like any island. Raiders are gated by an innermost raider-free ring (`WorldSettings.raiderFreeRing` = 0.12), not by home. **Still to come (phases 2–5):** the "Home" tab becomes "your camps"; `VoyageManager` banks at whichever of your piers you're alongside; the minimap colours camps; the home flag/statics get deleted; the authored plateau stays as one authored island among several; probes get pruned. §1–9 above are edited to stop asserting a home; the dated entries below this one are historical and carry `[superseded 2026-09-22: no home island]` instead of being rewritten.
  - **Trees stay felled across a load; regrowth comes from the far edge.** Kevin: *"the trees seem to appear anew after loading in... they should re-grow further away from camp, to help the camp not get overgrown."* The felled set is the nearest-to-campfire-first prefix of a deterministic order; the wood mesh redraws from the ledger on every arrival; `treesRegrown` accrues at the timber regrowth rate (2% of the felled count a day) and shortens the prefix from the FAR end, so whatever comes back is always furthest from the fire first. A 30 m clearing round the campfire never regrows while the fire stands.
  - **Ground foliage thinned and cleared under buildings.** Kevin: *"grows through buildings and makes the camp seem busy."* 35–40% smaller, half as dense, and cleared under every building footprint.
  - **Camp reach follows the campfire, not the ship.** The 80 m siting radius is measured from the campfire once it stands; before that, from the ship.
  - **Herds anchor on the island's own outline**, not a disc round its centre — crescents and spits had none. Sandbars with no ground above the beach keep only gulls.
  - **Night is never pitch black, and coastal shoal colour follows the time of day** (being built now; not shipped by this entry).
  - Kevin: a quarry turns rough stone into bricks for future building upgrades.
  - Kevin: "a fletcher's building for bow and arrow." 18 timber, 2 stone; a fletcher takes one log a day and makes three arrows (`BuildPlan.yieldPerInput`, 1 everywhere else). Arrows are spent, not sold: a hunter with arrows kills half again as many and spends one per animal, only as far as the quiver reaches; a posted lookout with arrows looses up to five at a raid, each arrow off the away-raid's share by a tenth (0.4 to 0.2), or two arrows drop one raider from a live landing party before he reaches the beach. The bow is the fletcher's own.
- 2026-09-22 — **The island loop got its close, its teeth, its beasts and a boat that fits.** All placeholder numbers, none played; details per section in `docs/PLAN-island-outposts.md`.
  - **The felt close.** A camp tells you what it did while you were gone (`OutpostLedger.Absence`, a card on arrival); the sheet and the yard say where the voyage is pointed (`Ship/TargetLine`: next rung against banked + aboard + piled, days at the camps' summed make-rate); each pile shows its ±/day.
  - **Upkeep consequences, as settled 2026-09-13.** The campfire is the provisions gauge (three days of food = bright); hands slide toward angry while unfed and work less below half mood, except when bringing in food; nobody leaves. **[2026-10-02: assignments are permanent, Age-of-Empires style — mood and hunger never stop work and nobody pouts; see the 2026-10-02 decisions-log entry "Assignments are permanent".]**
  - **Raids are LIVE, at Kevin's call.** *"more interactive, to happen when I as the player is there."* 25 s after you land at a camp with piles, the island's raider (`EnemyShip.Duty.Raid`) runs for the beach, lands a party that steals a unit at a time from the piles while you fight the ship; sink her and the loot comes back. The **watchtower** carries a cannon that fires while a lookout is on watch, and raiders shoot towers first. The away-clock raid stays until Kevin sets the frequency — *"which it should, to incentivize building up defences."*
  - **Raiders wear the fleet's five mid hulls**, red with white sails, by ring from home (nodes 7–11). Eight mid hulls are fine on the desktop; the phone budget is unmeasured.
  - **The player ship is a 13.5 m paddle launch for now** — the 32 m stern-wheeler at a uniform 0.42 (`SteamerBootstrap.PlaytestScale`; hull form scaled with the mesh, so she floats on her marks; top speed as √k; roll period ~5 s). Kevin: *"a good game testing ship."* Crew are stationed inside her from the hull form. Regenerate at real launch proportions once the size has been played.
  - **Wild animals.** Goats on rock, boar in green, gulls over every shore and three following the ship's wake. Herds graze, wander, flee crew and the ship, rest at night; 300 m LOD. Kevin *"loves the birds"*; goat and boar had a second modelling pass.
  - **Hunting.** `Res.Game`, a stock counted in animals; a hunter takes 0.5 a day, four Food each; the ledger kills and the scene agrees (`SyncHunting`), the worker mimes. Works on any island with a herd — including the starting island, which keeps no special rules. [superseded 2026-09-22: no home island]
- 2026-09-21 — **Huts fill themselves.** GDD 6, Upkeep: "huts cap supported hands ... neglected hands get angry" — this lands the capping and the growing, not the anger yet. `BuildPlan.houses` (Hut = 2, everything else 0) sums into `OutpostLedger.HousingCapacity`; every hand ashore eats `EatPerHandPerDay` (1) Food a day out of the pile inside `Step`, per quantum so D2 holds, and an unfed quantum banks its debt in `hungerDays` rather than starving or evicting anybody (parked for the neglect/anger pass). While there is a free bed AND at least `RecruitFoodCost` (3) Food in the pile, `recruitProgress` accrues; every `DaysPerRecruit` (3) it spends the 3 Food and a new `OutpostHand` is born — `born = true`, a name from `VillagerNames` picked deterministically off the camp's key and roster size, no ship body yet. All placeholder numbers, none played.
- 2026-09-21 — **Wheat on the island is gathered by hand; a farm is wheat that grows by the camp.** The Farm plan (`BuildPlans.Farm`) is the sawmill's shape — a `farmhand` position, `makes = Food`, one Work order with `target = "Farm"` — with no `takes`: the field is the input. The field is on the plan: `beds = 6`, `unitsPerBed = 4`, `bedRegrowPerDay = 0.25`, `bedSpacing = 1.8`. Raising a farm calls `OutpostLedger.AddField(plan)` → `AddStanding(Food, 24, 0.25)`: 24 Food standing, planted and ready, growing back 6 a day. A farmhand harvests `OutpostLedger.FoodPerHandPerDay` (6) a day off the standing Food into the camp stores under the same ceiling as everything else, so one farm keeps exactly one hand busy and a second one strips it in four days. A ledger with no Food stock (bare probe farm, older save) is unbounded as before. Path-independent (D2): regrowth and harvest are both per-quantum. `BuildingFactory` renames the kit farm's crop modules `Bed_00..` and its planting empties `BedSlot_00..` (`BedsOf` / `BedSlotsOf`); `farm_01` has four, `farm_02` six. Nothing eats Food yet. **All guesses, none played.**
_Append-only._
- **Sheet HUD (built the same day, first cut).** Kevin's verdict on the old HUD: unreadable, too big, badly organised. New rule: *one object, one sheet, one decision*. Nothing is drawn over the world; tap the campfire, a build site, the ship or a hand and a parchment sheet unfolds beside it, ring on the ground, and closes on X, on tapping ground, or when the job ends. Camp sheet carries stores with rates, what is going up, orders and the hands; manifest carries hold, per-kind stop-at, deck cargo, repair and the crew columns; site sheet carries progress and hands; hand sheet the verb lists. New player knobs: rations (full/half/none, eats at 1/0.5/0 per hand per day, half grumbles), work priority (food first / timber first, ×1.25 / ×0.8), the lookout's effect on the raid clock stated on the sheet, and a stop-at cap per resource when loading. Sailing HUD unchanged for now; the old camp panels are muted while anchored at a camp. Style is a placeholder Kevin will retune. **The chart** (same day): the square minimap, compass tape and nav line are replaced by one round chart instrument top-left: compass rim with the weather in its true bearing, a brass course pointer to the nearest own pier, islands drawn only once seen (hatched when glimpsed from ~700 m, ink once landed), camps as flames (fed / hungry / raided since you left), raiders with their patrol ring. Ship-up under way, north-up at anchor. Tap it for the chart sheet: every seen island, camp ledger lines, last day's track, set course. Discovery and the track are saved, so a full chart is something the player earned. Sheets are pinned to the right edge (Kevin: they must not move with the camera).

- 2026-09-22 — **One sheet, tabs: the standard sheet frame.** Kevin, after three mockups on the phone, picked option A. Every sheet now occupies the **same fixed region** of the screen and is divided the same way: a header that does not scroll, a tab strip, a scrolling body, and an action row pinned to the bottom of the frame. **The card never resizes itself to its content** — that was the thing he rejected, because a sheet that grows is a sheet whose buttons move under the thumb. Geometry (`SheetHost.Place`): upright, the **bottom third of the safe area**, full width, 12 px inner margins — at 1080x2340 that is x 12..1068, y 1572..2328 (1056 x 756). Landscape, the same content as a column **one third of the width docked right**, full safe height — at 1920x1080 that is x 1292..1908, y 12..1068 (616 x 1056). The frame reports itself to the IMGUI HUD as `SheetHost.FrameRect` / `HudLayout.ClaimSheet`, and `HudLayout.AvoidSheet` (inside `Declare`, so every rect the HUD issues passes through it) pushes anything that would land on it up (upright) or left (landscape); the ashore rail steps left of the column on a desk. The camp sheet is four tabs — **camp** (stores, what is going up, the four orders), **hands · N** (the roster), **build** (the plan list), **ship** (the manifest, embedded from `ShipSheet` rather than copied) — so "Raise a building" and "Load ship" stop being buttons that unfold the card. Hand sheet: orders / about, with *stand down* and *back aboard* pinned. Ship sheet: hold / crew, with *everyone ashore* and *cast off* pinned. Site and chart sheets have one section each and therefore **no tab strip** — a strip with one tab is a label pretending to be a control. Tabs are 44 px tall, the live one marked with the sheet's own accent (ember for the fire, sea for the ship and the chart, timber for a site, brass for a hand), and the last tab per sheet **type** is remembered for the session, so coming back down the beach lands you on the build list you left open. Nothing was dropped: every line and every button the sheets had is still there, on one of the tabs.
- 2026-09-22 (same day, after the phone) — **No scrolling: pages you swipe between.** Kevin, from the phone: *"When on campfire menu you need to scroll down to see all the options and that's a huge no no. All the information should be available on screen. If it doesn't fit you can swipe left to right for new 'windows'."* So the frame's scroll view is gone. The body is a **horizontal pager**: the tab strip's entries ARE the pages, a drag on the body past 20 px of horizontal intent turns the page with the finger, and past a quarter of the body's width (or a flick over 600 px/s) it commits — otherwise it snaps back. A vertical drag is never swallowed. **Every page is built to the band, and the band is arithmetic, not a guess** (`SheetHost.BandHeight`): the frame's height from the safe area, less the card border (4), the header (56), the strip (45), the action row (61) and the body's padding (26), all in panel units — 188 units of chrome, always subtracted even on a sheet that shows no strip, so a one-page sheet that grows a second page does not suddenly overflow. At 1080x2340 with the panel at 1:1 that leaves **564 units** for a page; on the phone's real panel scale it is nearer 300, which is exactly why the camp had to scroll. At 1920x1080 the right-hand column leaves **864**. Rows are a fixed 40 px (`SheetKit.RowPx`) and the rows-per-page falls out of the band — never hardcoded, so a page holds more on a desk than on a phone by construction. **Anything that can overflow pages itself**: the roster ("hands 1/2"), the build list, the manifest's stop-at list and its crew columns. The camp page carries the piles and "what is going up"; the four standing orders (rations, priority, the watch, the next hand) ride with them where the band is tall enough and become their own **orders** page where it is not — the watchtower's `FireSheet.FocusLookout` resolves to whichever page that is. **Five tabs is the strip's limit**; past that it becomes the compact strip — ‹ , the live page's name, a dot per page, › — because six 44 px tabs across a phone is six targets nobody can hit. Nothing was dropped: every line and every button is still there, on some page, and the page per sheet type is still remembered for the session.
- 2026-09-22 — **Tapping a villager opens his sheet, and the camera follows him.** Kevin: *"when pressing on the villager his information / commands I can give him should be the 1/3 screen UI"* — tapping his token in the crew list opened the hand sheet, tapping the man himself did nothing. The crew carry **no colliders** (`Hand.PickAt` is projection, not physics, on purpose), so `WorldPicker`'s raycast could only ever hit the beach he was standing on. The picker now asks `Hand.PickAt(screen, forPickup: false)` **first** — the same screen-space, thumb-sized radius the hand cursor uses, so the man the cursor highlights and the man the tap opens are the same man — and falls back to the raycast for everything that does have a collider. And because a hand walks off while you are reading his orders, `Sheets.Open` puts `IslandCam` on him (`Follow` / `StopFollow`) for as long as a hand sheet is the open sheet.


- 2026-09-21 — **A pier is buildable.** Kevin: *"I'd like a pier asset to be buildable to make it easier to dock with the island."* `BuildKind.Pier` / `BuildPlans.Pier`: 8 timber (5 under the playtest cap), 3 m wide, 14 m as priced, nobody works at it, keeps nothing. **The player chooses only WHERE along the beach**: `Outpost.SnapPier` walks the pick to the waterline, points the planks straight out to sea (down the height gradient), lands 3 m up the beach and runs out — 14 m, up to 24 — until there is 2.5 m of water under the sea end; a beach that never gets there is refused ("the water here is too shallow for a pier"). R does not turn it. The deck is 1.2 m above MEAN WATER, not above the ground. The chosen length rides in the ledger row (`PendingBuild.length` / `BuiltBuilding.length`) so a save brings it back the same. A raised pier carries a `Pier` component (`SeaEnd`, `Heading` land→sea, `Berth` 4 m off its right side at the seaward third) and reports itself through `Outpost.RegisterPierDock` / `UnregisterPierDock` — the dock registry is wired there, not called from the outpost.
- 2026-09-21 — **There is a save now: one JSON file, New or Continue on launch, the ledger unchanged.** Kevin: *"I need to be able to save in game and, when launching the game again, press New or Load."* `Scripts/Save/` — `SaveData` (JsonUtility: `[Serializable]`, lists, no polymorphism), `SaveGame` (capture, write, read, and a restore coroutine), `GameBoot` (the overlay). Detail in `PLAN-island-outposts.md`, "Save and load".
  - **D4 held.** The `OutpostLedger` went into the file as it was. The two things it lacked were **where each building stands** (a `raised` list of `{planId, x, z, yaw}`, written by `Outpost.Raise`, because the spiral would have moved every hut on load) and the camp centre, which was private.
  - **The world is seeded now.** `WorldSettings.seed` was 0, so island kinds, props, reefs and raiders re-rolled every launch and a camp keyed to a spice island could have come back on a stone one. It is 260921; the save carries it and refuses another seed's file, falling back to New with a warning.
  - **Restore order is a rule:** clock first (or every ledger pays a phantom backlog), then rung → fittings → bays, then hold and stores, then the pose, then outposts (survey, `Adopt`, `Rehome` the bodies by name), then the anchor last so the camp is awake to see her.
  - **Probes never touch the player's file.** `RunProbe.Call` dismisses the overlay through `GameBoot.Skip`, which keeps the file and switches autosaves off for the session. `SaveProbe` round-trips through a temp path.
